**Round ay (2026-10-25, the regular orchestrator):** **T30b.8 (PR #66) reviewed; merge when its CI is green.** The executor's four new readings were re-disassembled and hold: (a) the score tree inserts a node left only when its score is strictly lower (`jae` → right, `0x1000d501`) and fn1 pops the leftmost, so the lowest score pops first and equal scores first-in first-out; (b) `0x10002b80` sets the search origin `Y + 0x4ec` / `+0x4f0` to `max(rect.left, island.x)` / `max(rect.top, island.y)` and clips the far edges to the island (`0x10002bb5..0x10002c1b`, island getters `+0x50..+0x5c`), so chunk coordinates are relative to **the search rectangle clipped to the island**, in world tiles — this corrects the orchestrator's own §3.4.15 "island-relative" (true only when the search covers the island's top-left corner); (c) island fill mode 1 writes `8 + bit` per subcell into a 3×3 byte grid (`0x1009206c..0x100920e2`); (d) the goal test calls the job's callback per goal subcell in the order of the 9-entry row of `0x100c1160 + 36·row` and returns the AND of the answers (`0x100036fb..0x1000376a`). **The rule T33 implements is `state.md` §5.9 from "What T33 implements".** Two design decisions, taken here (neither changes the game's feel): (1) **the walk tables come from the game's own `AnnoWelt.dll` at run time** — the mapper (512 u16 at RVA `0xc1af8`), the five layer blocks (`0x1c00` bytes each at RVA `0xd1140 + 0x1c00·L`) and the goal-order table (5 × 9 × 4 bytes at RVA `0xc1160`) are original data and may not be copied into our source (CLAUDE.md: the original files are required at run time, never redistributed); `formats` reads them field by field from the PE file like `Anlagen.dat`, and the sim receives them as immutable data in the type registry's neighbourhood (no I/O in `sim`); (2) **the shore stays a documented stand-in** until `0x10081f51` is read: a tile is closed (mask `0x1ff`) when the island's terrain says it is water by the original's own whole-tile test (`0x10092b40`: the mean of the four corner heights below the threshold, T33.2 reads the threshold's initialiser `0x1008ff00`), and the per-subcell coast pass `0x10081ce0` is not applied — this changes only coast tiles and is listed as a deviation; **Round as (2026-10-25, the regular orchestrator):** **T15.B1b (PR #62) stopped correctly** — the task's pattern was wrong, not the sweep: the game does not compare virtual-key codes in a message handler, it **polls** them. `AnnoFrame.dll` imports `GetAsyncKeyState` (IAT slot `0x10086208`, `analysis/pe_symbols.json`), and its only two users are **`0x10005a10`** and **`0x1000b090`** (D, re-disassembled): each loads the import into `edi`, calls it for `0x26` (up → bit 4), `0x28` (down → bit 8), `0x25` (left → bit 1), `0x27` (right → bit 2), and if any is down computes a step from the frame's argument `[esp + 0x28]` / `[esp + 0x34]` (capped at `0x32` = 50 ms, like the tick loop's cap) times the float field `[this + 0x15f4]` times the constant at `0x1008641c`, divided through, then multiplied by `0x10086418` (horizontal) or `0x10086414` (vertical), and calls **`0x10006850` (left), `0x100068d0` (right), `0x10006950` (up), `0x100069d0` (down)** with that float, `this = esi` (the same object; `0x1000b090` also reads `[this + 0x1e7c]` as a pre-set direction mask and skips when `[this + 0x16fc] == −1`). So the camera's scroll methods and their object are named; T15.B1b's steps 3–5 continue as **T15.B1c** (below). PR #62 merges as the record when green. **Lane:** T15.B1c → T30b.3o → T30b.3h2 → T30b.5–8.

**Round ap (2026-10-25, the regular orchestrator):** **T30b.3m (PR #59) reviewed; merge when its CI is green.** Re-checked with `pefile` / `disasm.py`: `0x100c14e4 + 0x50` = `0x10002b80` and `+ 0x80` = `0x1000dd20` (so both run with `this = Y + 4`); `0x1000dd20` computes `0x100d1140 + 0x1c00·arg0` into `[this + 0x430]` = `Y + 0x434` — **the expansion's byte table is the figure's layer block, D**; `0x10002b80` stores arg2 at `[this + 0x2040]` = `Y + 0x2044` (the island mode) and the island at `Y + 0x438` (the `0x10002b9e` store I named in round ao was the island, not the table); `0x10003aa0` returns the start cell's edge-connectivity bits `0x1000/0x2000/0x4000/0x8000` (its tail `or esi, 0x2000` / `or esi, 0x4000` re-read), OR-ed into the start cell's low u16 at `0x10003f73` — the nibble question is closed: only start cells carry it, every other popped node is a reached cell with a came-from code and takes a special expansion; the disc pass closes the cells **outside** the allowed disc (code 7, admission 0 in all five blocks), correcting my round-ao reading; admission bytes `0x80..0x86` are 0 in all five blocks. All D with addresses, the report precise, the tool extension (`layer_blocks.py --disk-summary`) small. **One reading to carry into T30b.3n (P, from the admission groups the report lists):** for tile bytes `128..255` the admission byte depends only on `(code − 128) >> 3` through the 16 values `0, 64, 16, 112, 4, 68, 28, 124, 1, 193, 17, 241, 7, 199, 31, 255` — the four group bits look like the tile's four orthogonal connections (up = 64, right = 16, down = 4, left = 1) with the diagonal bits (32, 128, 8, 2) admitted when both neighbouring orthogonals are set; so a road tile's byte would carry its connection pattern in bits 3–6 and the walk rule for roads is 'follow the connections'. T30b.3n should test this against what the tile writer stores for the straight / corner / junction road pieces. **Two consequences for the plan:** (1) the executor is right that the four special expansions read different low-u16 bit groups (`0x10007b2e`, `0x100077ae`, `0x10007ed6`, `0x100071f3`) and do turn-cost arithmetic (`0x1000723d..0x10007253`), so T33 cannot treat them as 'default minus one direction': **T30b.3o** (below) reads them to their `ret`; (2) `0x10008230`'s constraint mask (fed into the pre-loop AND at `0x10004737`) is open and goes into T30b.3o too. The four stores, with each `push` accounted for, actually land `(x0, y0, x1, y1)` = `(edi, edx, edi + 2, edx + 2)`: `0x100011fa [esp + 0x14] = edi` at frame X−0x24 writes buffer+0 = x0; the intervening `push 0` shifts to frame X−0x28; `0x1000121e [esp + 0x1c] = edx` writes buffer+4 = y0; `0x10001225 [esp + 0x20] = edi + 2` writes buffer+8 = x1; `0x10001229 [esp + 0x24] = edx + 2` writes buffer+0xc = y1. The §3.3.2 §-level conclusion holds: the yes/no decision lives either in the search engine (world `+0x6aa8`, vtable `0x100c1424`, OQ-161) that consumes the sub-cell marks `0x1000dd60` / `0x10003780` write, or in the figure's own tile tests, or in a cross-module consumer not yet surveyed. **T30b.3d opened as the next sub-task** (one of the three paths §3.3.2 named for OQ-175 and already READY on the board): cross-module `--reloc` sweep on `AnnoGame.dll` / `AnnoScene.dll` / `AnnoDisplay.dll` for `0x100d1140..0x100d9d40` and the five block-base immediates — small, docs-only, and decisive if the hits are zero (confirms the "layer tables may be dead" theory before T30b.4 goes to Wine). **T30b.4 (the Wine memory compare)** stays `READY` and PARALLEL-OK with T30b.3d — different sections, different files. The live-lane order §2 keeps: **T30b.3d first** (one sub-task at a time; smaller, faster, and sets T30b.4's framing). Lesson for the executor (same as round ag, now a protocol invariant): **the order of 4-dword buffer stores is determined by where `esp` is at each store, not by the order of `add eax, N` / `add ecx, N` arithmetic steps between stores.** Every store between a `push` and the next instruction uses a frame shifted by 4; label stores by frame-aware buffer offset, not by the register name's position in an `add` chain.

(2) The 4-dword buffer tuple "`{ox + x + 4, ox + x, oy + y + 4, oy + y}`" was wrong order — the four stores land `{ox + x, oy + y, ox + x + 4, oy + y + 4}` = `(x0, y0, x1, y1)` (the SAME shape §3.3 names for `0x1000daf0`), traceable instruction by instruction with each `push edx` shifting `esp`; the executor paired the `+4`'s together, likely from the `add` instructions rather than from where the stores actually land. Neither error changes the headline; both are "D" claims that were factually wrong, so they got the orchestrator's correction in place rather than another sub-task. Nit (not corrected, acceptable as written): the executor says "`0xffffffff`-ish image data" for the first dword of every layer block; L=3's first dword is `0x0000ffff` and L=5's is `0x00000000`, so "`0xffffffff` or `0x00000000`" would be more accurate; neither is a valid vtable, which is the point. **T30b.3c opened (below)** to read `0x100011c0` to its `ret` — the other `[reg + 0x430]` pointer-consumer the sweep now finds — and decide whether it is the same class as `0x10003780`'s receiver, or a different one. **T30b.4 (the Wine memory compare)** can run in parallel with T30b.3c: with the "layer tables may not be consumed in `AnnoWelt.dll` at all" theory now the leading hypothesis, the Wine compare's target shifts from "the layer-block contents are static" to **"the layer-table POINTER `[X + 0x430]` is set and then never dereferenced on class X at run time"**. Lesson for the executor: a `ret N` on an x86 function reads straight off the function's own epilogue (`c2 NN NN`), not the push count of a call inside the function; and the order of 4-dword buffer stores depends on where `esp` is at each store, not on the order of `add eax, 4` / `add ecx, 4` steps.

If the `0x1000` group is one shape (a corner, a T, an end piece) the game must walk it, or no dirt road could turn: `0x1000` would then be a shape marker, not "no traffic". T30b.6 can tell from the opening's road tiles which ids a straight, a corner and a junction carry.

New test case, two mutants killed, one existing scheduler case rebuilt with its own layout (asserts untouched); no hash moved; 974/974 ctest.

The stand-in's `kRoadFlag` test (bit 14) only matches 26 of the 36 named-road variants in `Anlagen.dat`; the other 10 carry bit 12 (`0x1000`) instead. **30 073 of 39 161 cart searches** in `S01_07_01` are blocked by exactly three dirt-road / cobblestone-street variants. `TransportStats` extended with per-run counts through a `TickContext*` sink (not state).
  - **Round docs:** rounds v, w, x, y, z committed to `main` (`62d4e68`, `c41d63d`, `bca27a8`, `e2be853`, this one).
- **Open, awaiting the orchestrator's and the owner's reads:**
  - **T30 PR #43**, head **ce97d51** (CI 20/20): partial RE of the walking layers. Two headlines in the diff:
    - **Step 5 (D, named-road puzzle, round x finding confirmed and extended):** 36 named-road `Anlagen.dat` entries fall into 26 with HEAD `flags_word & 0x4000` (kRoadFlag) and 10 with `0x1000` instead (bits 14 and 12 never set together). Every named-road WALKMASK is 12 zero bytes; 10 bridge "transition" pieces carry per-cell WALKMASK with sub-footprint walkable patterns.
    - **OQ-175 (round z, deeper RE):** the layer-table reader is a **family of three variants** at different offsets on their `this` (`0x1000daf0`, `0x1000ed40`, `0x1000f9d0`); the twin engine (vtable `0x100c1a30`) is embedded at `+0x23a4` of a larger outer engine (constructor `0x10012079`). The 9-bit mask mapper `0x100c1af8` has **33 distinct outputs** over 512 inputs (byte offsets into the 7168-byte layer block, not aligned to 8/16); `0xfff` dominates with 123 of 512 masks ("blocked / default", likely). The 33 cell-types × 8 bytes = ~264 bytes of real data in a 7168-byte block, the rest slack. **Still open:** the body of `[edx + 0xec]` on `[this + 0x858]` (the yes/no), and no direct write to `[reg + 0x858]` was found in the sweep — likely set by a later `init` call on the engine. Four of the 76 `call [reg + 0xec]` sites in `AnnoWelt.dll` match this family's push pattern.

## 1. Protocol for the executor

1. **Read first, every session:** `CLAUDE.md`, this file, `docs/PLAN.md` §0.1–0.3 and §7, and the spec named by the task. The rules in `CLAUDE.md` apply unchanged (evidence only, determinism, no hardcoded limits, tests before refactors, `game/` read-only, nothing from `game/` in git).
2. **Pick the first task whose status is `READY`** and whose dependencies are `DONE`; tasks marked `PARALLEL-OK` may run at the same time as another task in a separate git worktree (`git worktree add ../anno1503-<id> -b task/<id>-<slug> main`). Never run two tasks that touch the same files in parallel.
3. **One branch per task:** `task/<id>-<slug>` from `main`. Small conventional commits (`feat(render): …`, `fix(sim): …`, `docs: …`, `test(...)`), one concern each, each ending with the attribution line the harness gives you. **Do not merge into `main` and do not push** unless the task says so. The orchestrator merges after review.
4. **Gates before you report a task as finished** (all of them; the owner's PC has 16 threads, use them):
   - `cmake --build --preset msvc` warning-free (warnings are errors).
   - `$env:ANNO1503_GAME_DIR="$PWD\game\app"; $env:ANNO_TEST_THREADS=16; ctest --preset msvc -j 16` green **and** the same without `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR` (skips must be clean skips).
   - clang-format 23.1.3 clean: `python -m pip install --target <scratch>/cfpkg clang-format==23.1.3` then `find src -name '*.cpp' -o -name '*.hpp' | xargs <scratch>/cfpkg/bin/clang-format.exe --dry-run --Werror` (a venv fails with Python 3.14 in Git Bash).
   - For `sim`/`formats`/`test_support` changes: the GCC 13.3 `-O3 -Werror` check in WSL (`Ubuntu`) with the CI's exact flags (`-O3 -DNDEBUG -std=c++20 -Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic -Wconversion -Wsign-conversion -Wshadow -Wnon-virtual-dtor -Wold-style-cast -Wcast-align -Wnull-dereference -ffp-contract=off -Werror`, doctest from `build/msvc/_deps/doctest-src`, run with and without `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR`). For `render`/`app` changes it is enough that the MSVC build is clean and you avoided the known GCC traps (`std::swap`/copy of structs holding `std::optional<std::vector<…>>` at -O3; sign conversions).
   - `tools/agree.py` where an `anno-dump` command changed.
   - A performance task also needs its `bench_frame` / `bench_sim` numbers in `docs/perf/README.md` (p50/p95/p99/max, hardware, commit).
5. **Subagents:** use them only for independent `PARALLEL-OK` tasks in their own worktrees, one subagent per task and never more than the board assigns. **No reviewer subagents (owner instruction 2026-10-23 s):** the independent review is the orchestrator's; the executor runs the gates and its own mutation checks (flip a constant or a branch, the named test must fail; list them in the report) before reporting. Earlier rule, for the record (rounds up to r): two read-only reviewers per finished task.
6. **Report** by appending a section to `REPORTS.md` in the format given there: what was built, evidence (test names, numbers, PNG paths under `C:\Users\altin\Pictures\anno-review\<id>\`), what the reviewers found and what you changed, what is left, open questions. Then stop that task; **do not start the next step of a task marked `OWNER-CHECK` until this file says the owner has looked**.
7. **Decisions you must not make:** anything that changes the game's look or feel (defaults of render styles, river tuning, rules options, limits) is the owner's (`docs/open-questions.md`, "Decisions for the owner"). Implement the faithful or stated default behind an option and list the question in your report. Obvious bugs you just fix.
8. **If blocked** (missing information, a gate that cannot pass for a reason outside the task, a conflict with `main`): write a `BLOCKED` entry in `REPORTS.md` with what you tried and move to the next `READY` task.
9. **Linux and macOS warnings are part of every gate, not only for sim/formats.** `cmake/AnnoWarnings.cmake` turns `-Wsign-conversion`, `-Wnull-dereference`, `-Wconversion`, `-Wshadow`, `-Wold-style-cast` into errors on GCC and Clang; MSVC does not report most of them. Before a report says "warning-free", the executor runs the GCC 13 `-O3 -Werror` build in WSL over every changed library and its tests (render and app included) and a Clang build of the same; the orchestrator does not merge a branch whose CI run on the branch (push it, open a PR) is not green. Learned 2026-10-21: T1c and T1e were merged with `main` red for a day. **GPU tests skip in CI** (until T13): a render report states which GPU tests ran locally and on which adapter(s); a GPU change without that line is not reviewed.
10. **Run what you built and look at it yourself before you report (owner instruction 2026-10-21 o).** Tests are not enough: the owner found the pixelated far zoom of T3 stage 3 in the first minute of looking. For every change that affects what is drawn: run the viewer or `--png` over the views the change touches (every zoom level it affects, both water styles, the ten `0200Single` scenarios plus the canyon island `D_256x256_0045_5B.scp` where the change is not local to one place, 1280x800 and 2560x1440, the RTX 4090 and the Radeon iGPU where the GPU path changed), open the PNGs (the Read tool shows images) and look for pixelation, doubled rows, seams, holes, pop-in, wrong colours or depth order, and read the viewer's frame log for hitches. Where the original can show the same view (T15's harness once it exists), compare against it. For simulation changes, run the original's fixtures once T15 stage 1 has them. Say in the report what you ran and what you saw; the owner's look comes after yours.

11. **Sub-tasks, one PR each (since round aa; the executor is a Nemotron 3.5 Super VL model, no subagents).** An open task is split on this board into numbered sub-tasks (`T30b.1`, `T30b.2`, …). Take the first sub-task whose status is `READY` and whose predecessors are `DONE` or `REVIEW`; do only that sub-task; open its PR; write its report section; then stop and take the next one. Never two at once, no subagents. Each sub-task's steps are ticked in order; a step's result is written down before the next step starts (a table or a list in the report, not from memory at the end). **Thoroughness over speed:** a sub-task has no time box; re-read a disassembly twice before tagging a claim D; a number is re-computed before it is reported; a claim about the original carries module + address, and the report says which instructions were read (address range) — never the disassembly text itself, never bytes of the original. If a step does not hold, write what was found and the question into REPORTS.md and stop that step (item 7 below). Before calling a stack slot "beyond the arguments" or a value "untraceable", count the pushes that are live at that instruction and read the function's only caller (round ae: `[esp + 0x14]` in `0x1000eb40` was its second argument, the world). **This-adjustment rule (round an):** every class of this engine has several vtables (interfaces at `+0`, `+4`, `+8`, class Y also at `+0x2048`); a method's `this` is the address of the vtable slot it was called through, so `[this + k]` in a method of the `+4` interface is `[Y + 4 + k]`. Before you compare a field of one method with a field of another, write both as absolute `Y + n` (first row of every function table: "this = Y + ?, because slot … of vtable … at `[Y + ?]`", or "called with `lea ecx, [reg + 4]` where reg = …"). Docs-only sub-tasks need no build; a sub-task that adds a tool under `tools/` runs it once and pastes the command and its first lines of output (counts and names only) into the report. (Two items of this list carry the number 7; "item 7" in the task sections means the steps rule below.)

7. **Steps, not judgement calls (since round u; the executor is a simpler model).** A task section lists numbered steps with the files, the functions and the commands. Do the steps in order; do not add scope, do not refactor what a step does not name, do not change a default the owner has to decide (`rules` options, OQ decisions). **Allowed without asking (owner, 2026-10-24 w), when listed in the report:** tests for the code the step changed, and fixes of sentences (docs, comments) that would otherwise contradict that code. Anything else is a question. When a step is unclear, when a file or function it names does not exist, or when a measurement contradicts the step's premise: stop that step, write the question with what you found into REPORTS.md under the task, and continue with the next step that does not depend on it. Report numbers as measured, including the ones that miss the acceptance. Every claim about the original carries module + address, every mapping in code cites its address in a comment. The acceptance list is ticked item by item in REPORTS.md with the evidence (the test name, the command's output line).

### T1 — Smooth zoom, step 1: no popping after a zoom step · `REVIEW` → continued as T1b (owner looked 2026-10-20: "better but not smooth, weird artifacts in enhanced mode")
- **Owner's complaint:** zooming in and out fast shows strange artifacts and feels clunky, like a performance gap; it must be smooth whatever the number of players or the progress of the game; and a larger zoom-out range is wanted.
- **What was measured (2026-10-20, `ANNO_VIEW_ZOOM_SCRIPT=4,12`, enhanced water):** the window thread is fine (worst busy frame 2.8 ms, 250–330 fps) but every zoom step triggers new chunk builds ("zoom group N: 1–3 chunk(s) received"), and the step is an instant 2× jump of the tile size (`kTile` = 84×42, 42×21, 20×10). So the artifacts are most likely content popping in after the jump plus the hard step itself. Not confirmed against what the owner sees.
- **Step 1 (this task):** after a zoom step, chunks of the new group that are not built yet must not show as empty/sea: draw the previous group's chunk content for that screen area, scaled by the tile ratio (2× or 0.5×), until the new chunk arrives, then swap. GPU path first (it is the viewer's default); the CPU path may keep the current behaviour if the cost is out of proportion, say so. Keep the scaling in the compositor (`src/render/src/gpu.cpp` / `gpu.wgsl`), not in the viewer. The swap must not produce a visible seam between a stand-in chunk and a built neighbour (same world alignment).
- **Before coding:** read `docs/render/iso.md` ("Scene composition", "Fidelity"), `docs/perf/README.md` ("2026-10-19 — zoom stutter"), `src/render/include/anno/render/frame.hpp`, `gpu.hpp`, `Viewer::zoom`. Also decide OQ-077 ("missing chunks: open sea or a stand-in from the nearest built zoom group") in the faithful-neutral way: stand-in from the nearest built group is this step; record the decision note in `docs/open-questions.md` OQ-077 as "implemented as stand-in, owner to confirm".
- **Scalability requirements:** no per-frame CPU work proportional to the number of chunks on screen beyond what exists; the stand-in lookup is O(visible chunks); no new fixed limits (group count stays `kGroups`-driven, nothing assumes exactly three groups; a comment where the 2× ratio is derived from `kTile`, not written as a literal).
- **Evidence to produce:** `bench_frame --zoom 6 --renderer gpu --chunks async --limits interactive` before/after (same p50/p95/worst, no new hitch lines); a PNG pair of the first frame after a zoom step before/after (`--png` with a scripted zoom, or a screenshot path) under `C:\Users\altin\Pictures\anno-review\T1\`; a doctest that composes a frame with one missing chunk of group 1 and a built group 2 and checks the stand-in pixels equal the 2×-scaled source within 1 per channel (CPU reference) and GPU = CPU within the existing tolerance.
- **Then stop** and report; the owner looks at the viewer. Steps 2–4 are T2–T4 and will be unlocked here one at a time.

### T1b — Stand-ins: find the unattributed hitch, separate the artifacts, redo the evidence · `REVIEW` → continued as T1c
- **Why:** the owner's run (RTX 4090, 1280×800, enhanced, `D_256x256_0045_5B`) shows a cluster of 26–39 ms busy frames right after the first zoom into an uncached group, and the viewer's own breakdown (chunks, uploads, sprites, encode, present) accounts for 2–3 ms of them. A 30 ms hole in the instrumentation is a defect on its own, and T1 added exactly the kind of work that could hide there. T1 is not merged until this is explained and fixed.
- **Do, in this order:**
  1. **Instrument, do not guess.** Time every window-thread step of a GPU frame and print the full sum in the `frame hitch:` line permanently, so busy − (sum of stages) is ≈ 0: `sync_chunks` split into drops (`drop_chunk` must still go to the reaper thread, verify), real uploads, and the `upload_chunk(key, nullptr)` placeholders (a zoom step makes every visible chunk missing at once: that is N texture + bind-group creations in one frame, check it), `stand_in_cover` and the piece building, `write_pieces` when it reallocates (a new buffer and bind group mid-frame), the wave-window setup after a zoom, and the time inside `wgpuQueueSubmit` / surface acquire. Reproduce with `ANNO_VIEW_ZOOM_SCRIPT` on the owner's island and with `bench_frame --zoom 6 --limits interactive`, stand-ins on and off in the same binary, **on an idle machine** (no other worktree building, no other benchmark; check load before each run).
  2. **Fix what the numbers name.** Likely candidates: placeholder creation per missing chunk (make it one shared placeholder bind group, or create lazily within the 1 ms upload budget); piece buffer sized once for the worst case (visible chunks × groups) instead of growing mid-frame; anything that frees GPU objects on the window thread.
  3. **Artifacts, enhanced mode.** The owner sees "weird graphics artifacts while zooming in enhanced mode". Three concrete suspects, test each with a scripted zoom and `--png`/`--zoom-dump` frames: (a) rivers on a stand-in are drawn with the legacy shimmer and flip to flow shading when the real chunk lands (your own report says so): either draw the flow from the source chunk's lattice scaled, or fade the swap; (b) the wave simulation window is rebuilt after a zoom and the coast shows analytic waves for 0.1–0.4 s (OQ-076, pre-existing): measure whether it is visible at the owner's zoom speed; (c) nearest-neighbour 2× stand-ins (blocky, then sharp) next to built neighbours (sharp): a visible checkerboard while chunks arrive one by one. Ask the owner, with your five-choice question, **and** give them the two switches to isolate it: `ANNO_VIEW_STAND_INS=off` and `--water original`.
  4. **Redo the evidence.** The bench numbers in the report were taken with 13–82 % background load from the other worktrees. Not accepted. Rerun on an idle machine, five alternated runs, stand-ins on/off same binary, groups 0/1/2, and update `docs/perf/README.md`; the after-numbers of p99/max (group 0: 2.9/8.6 → 5.3/19.2 ms, group 2: 3.4/7.0 → 9.1/19.1 ms) must either disappear or be explained. Regenerate the T1 PNG pairs after the per-part fix (the report says they predate it).
  5. **Close the `div_euclid` note.** The library function is correct for −512 / 512 (quotient −1, remainder 0, no adjustment). A result of −2 means the test fed another input or read another variable. Find it, state it in the report, remove "believed fine".
- **Acceptance:** hitch line sums to the busy time; no busy frame above 8 ms after a zoom step on the owner's island at 1280×800 and at 2560×1440 with enhanced water on the 4090 (apart from the very first frame of the program); the artifact the owner sees is either fixed or named with a PNG and an OQ for the owner; idle-machine bench table; gates green. Then `OWNER-CHECK` again.
- **Process note:** the report says no subagent tool was available and the reviewer findings "were supplied by the orchestrator". If the executor session cannot spawn agents, say so in the report header each time so the orchestrator runs the reviewers itself. Do not run more than one build or benchmark at a time on the owner's PC; benchmarks are invalid under load.

### T1c — Stand-ins: fix the review findings, then the idle bench and the owner check · `DONE` (reviewed and merged 2026-10-20; the owner looks at the `main` build, see §4)
- **Review verdict on f6be695 (independent reviewer, 2026-10-20):** the stand-in feature, the per-step instrumentation and the `--water enhanced` fix are sound (7 of 7 GPU-vs-CPU stand-in tests, 0 pixels differ; mapping derived from `kTile`; threads joined; no use-after-free found). Not mergeable because of the prewarm work added in T1b. Fix in this order, one commit each:
  1. **HIGH: priority inversion.** The prewarm thread creates textures at below-normal priority while holding wgpu-hal's device allocator lock (`gpu.cpp:376-423`, `thread_priority.hpp:33`); the window thread needs the same allocator for every upload and placeholder buffer. Under load (the owner's game at 50-82 %) the window thread waits a scheduling quantum, exactly in the frames after a zoom. Do not lower the prewarm thread's priority; refill the warm pool only in quiet frames (the window thread signals a frame with no uploads), or create planes on the window thread under a time budget at the end of quiet frames. Add `ANNO_VIEW_PREWARM=off` and `ANNO_BACKGROUND_PRIORITY=normal` switches to the viewer so the owner can A/B.
  2. **HIGH: commit 37d01c5 does not do what it says.** wgpu-native returns a non-null handle with an error id on a failed texture creation, so `if (!p.texture || !p.view) return;` (`gpu.cpp:415`) can never fire; a refused plane would enter the pool and every frame binding it fails validation (black viewer). Do not use error scopes from the background thread (the scope stack is per device and shared with the window thread's validation scope). Use the uncaptured-error counter delta around the creation, or stop prewarming at the first uncaptured error. Correct the claim in the perf README.
  3. **MEDIUM: `retain` runs every frame on every path** (`frame.cpp:646-658`), even with stand-ins off and on the CPU path; it is the +0.1-0.3 ms p50 and it changes `trim` for stand-ins-off, so "off = old behaviour" is not true. Gate it on missing chunks and a `FrameRenderer` flag set from `GpuLimits::stand_ins`.
  4. **MEDIUM: one placeholder buffer + bind group per missing visible chunk in one frame** (`gpu.cpp:843`, `:752-777`; 12-40 per zoom step by resolution) on the contended allocation path. Use a slab with dynamic offsets (the piece buffer already is one) or no placeholder at all when stand-in draws cover the chunk.
  5. **MEDIUM: VRAM bound.** The warm target (`prewarm_chunks * 5` = 120 planes) ignores `plane_pool` (up to `kPoolPlanes` = 192 idle planes): up to about 250 MB of textures that draw nothing, plus GPU chunks for the whole CPU cache of all groups, with no device-memory budget. Warm target = prewarm target minus pool size; derive `prewarm_chunks` from the visible chunk count of the largest group; put a VRAM budget into `GpuLimits` with a reasoned default (PLAN 0.1).
  6. **MEDIUM: OQ-094 rule incomplete, doc gap.** "busy" includes `present`; on NVIDIA Vulkan with the window composited on the iGPU the vsync/copy wait can land in `vkQueuePresentKHR`, so a display stall shows as a large `present` step inside busy. Add that to OQ-094 and the README; also say that the hitch line's own console write lands in the end-to-start gap of the next frame.
  7. **LOW, but test it first (hypothesis for the owner's 26-39 ms frames):** `zoom group N: k chunk(s) received` is written to stderr on the window thread once per frame while chunks arrive (`viewer.cpp:816`), after present, inside busy and outside every old stage. A synchronous console write with a game at 50-80 % CPU can cost tens of ms; `bench_frame` prints nothing per frame, which would explain why it was never reproduced. Verify with the owner's command to the terminal versus `2> hitch.log`; then rate-limit the line into the 2-second report.
  8. LOW: `take_warm` `try_lock` loses to the prewarm thread's `count_if` pick (hold the lock for push/pop only); add a water-field noise test for stand-ins (`sea_fs` through `chunk_local`, original water) since the current tests cover land only; document that `base` on a CPU stand-in is in source rows.
- Then: the idle-machine bench table (five alternated runs, stand-ins on/off, prewarm on/off, 1280×800 and 2560×1440; check load < 10 % before each run; the machine was idle at 20:30), `docs/perf/README.md` updated, gates, report. Acceptance as T1b. Then `OWNER-CHECK`.
- **Owner answer 2026-10-20 to the five-choice question: all of 1 to 4** (blank or flashing patches, hard rectangular cut-offs or seams, blurry/blocky areas turning sharp, shimmering or flickering water and rivers). Treat every one as a defect to remove (T1d), not as a taste question. In T1c step 7 and the idle runs, also capture frames of each with `--zoom-dump` so T1d starts from evidence; note which ones still appear with `ANNO_VIEW_STAND_INS=off` and with `--water original`.
- **Note on the owner's earlier observations:** commit 985a268 shows the viewer ignored `--water enhanced` until now; everything the owner saw "in enhanced mode" before was original water unless `E` was pressed. The owner's next look is the first that starts in enhanced water from the command line.

### T1d — Remove the four zoom artifacts · `DONE` (merged with T1e as 0467a51 on 2026-10-21)
- **Owner check of the merged `main` build (2026-10-20, enhanced water, canyon island):** "MUCH smoother and overall better now. When I'm just scrolling [zooming with the wheel] it's really smooth. River also looks perfect now." The artifacts appear **only when moving through the map while zooming** (pan and wheel at the same time). Read that as: a zoom step onto an area that was not visible before has no built chunk in any group, so there is no stand-in source and the chunk appears as open sea, then pops in; cross-fades (item 1) and a margin of pre-built chunks around the view in the current and the adjacent groups (T4, pulled forward into this task as item 5) are the fixes; the river flip (item 3) and the wave window (item 4) are no longer reported and move to the end, done only if the dumped frames still show them. **The owner's hitch log of this run:** no busy frame above 5.3 ms after program start; the old 26-39 ms clusters are gone. Two small items for T1d's measurements: `queue submit` spikes of 3.9-5.0 ms in single frames (named in T1b, cause unknown, GPU/driver work behind the submit) and one frame with 4.2 ms of window-event handling (a wheel burst? check what the event loop does per wheel event; it must not do per-event work proportional to anything). **Reproduce first:** `ANNO_VIEW_ZOOM_SCRIPT` with a pan (add a pan component to the script if it has none) and `--zoom-dump` frames.
- **Earlier owner decision 2026-10-20:** the owner saw all four artifact kinds while zooming in enhanced water and wants zooming "smooth whatever the load"; the decisions OQ-091, OQ-092 and OQ-076 are therefore taken as: no visible pop, flip or seam at any swap. Implement, one commit each, measured with the zoom script on an idle machine, GPU = CPU reference tests where a CPU reference exists:
  1. **Cross-fade on every chunk swap** (stand-in → real chunk, and open sea → first chunk): the compositor keeps the previous source for a chunk for about 150 ms and blends per pixel; duration a `GpuLimits` value with a reasoned default. This removes 1 (blank patches flashing) and 3 (blocky turning sharp) as hard transitions and softens 2.
  2. **Seams (2):** a stand-in part and a built neighbour must meet on the same world pixel; find why they do not (texel centre, the `dst` rectangle rounding, the half-texel at the part boundary, the `base` plane) with a synthetic noise test whose chunks straddle a stand-in boundary, and fix it in the shader and the CPU reference together. If the cut-offs are chunk-rectangle shaped and show open sea, that is 1, covered by the cross-fade plus T4's prebuild.
  3. **Rivers on a stand-in (4a, OQ-091):** draw the flow from the source chunk's lattice scaled by the tile ratio (the lattice textures exist per river chunk) so that the swap to the real chunk changes nothing visible; if the lattice resolution makes that impossible at 2×, cross-fade the shading.
  4. **Wave window after a zoom (4b, OQ-076):** hold the previous group's simulation window and keep drawing it rescaled until the new window has run its first steps, then cross-fade; never show the analytic waves or a diamond of restarted waves. The window rebuild stays off the window thread. (Only if the dumped frames still show it after items 1, 2 and 5.)
  5. **Margin of pre-built chunks (T4 pulled forward):** keep a ring of chunks around the view built in the current group and the adjacent groups, prioritised below the visible ones, bounded by the existing cache budget, so a pan-while-zooming lands on built or stand-in-covered content. Measure with the pan-and-zoom script on `Single_24.sze` (the largest scene): no chunk of the new view without a source in any group after the first second, and no busy frame above 8 ms.
- Acceptance: with `ANNO_VIEW_ZOOM_SCRIPT` on the owner's island, frame-to-frame pixel difference outside the legitimately animated water never jumps above a stated threshold at a swap (a test over dumped frames); no busy frame above 8 ms; the owner looks and reports which of 1-4 remain.
- T2 (animated step) and T3 (continuous zoom) follow; their cross-fades reuse item 1.

### T1e — Pan while zooming: fix the T1d review findings, then the owner check · `DONE` (verified in the diff by the orchestrator, owner check complete: "fade feels fine, chunks appear while paused"; merged into `main` as 0467a51 on 2026-10-21; see §4)
- **Why not merged:** two independent read-only reviewers (§4) found that the T1d code makes a *plain pan* worse: the fade starts for every chunk first uploaded while visible, and the current group's margin chunks are built ahead but never uploaded ahead, so every chunk that scrolls in at the screen edge dissolves for 150 ms from a 2x stand-in or the sea colour (`gpu.cpp` `if (limits.chunk_fade_ms > 0 && (it == chunks.end() || !it->second.real))`). The owner found plain scrolling "really smooth" on `main`; that must not regress. The `--pop` metric cannot show it (a dissolve lowers the per-frame jump). One commit per item, in this order:
  1. **No dissolve on a plain pan.** (a) `sync_chunks` uploads the built chunks of the *current* group's margin ahead too (the prefetch path, same per-frame limit and `prefetch_chunks` cap, visible chunks and the neighbouring groups' views ordered behind it as today), so a chunk is on the GPU before it enters the view; (b) the fade starts only when a placeholder (stand-in or open sea) was actually drawn for that key in an earlier frame (`it != chunks.end() && !it->second.real`, or a "shown as placeholder" mark), never for a chunk that appears at the screen edge. Measure: bench with a pan and no zoom (`--zoom 0 --zoom-pan 4,2`): `chunks_fading` stays 0 in every frame after the first second; `open_sea_chunks` 0.
  2. **Fade clock.** The fade runs on `render()`'s animation `time_ms`, which the viewer freezes on pause (`Viewer::time_ms`, `paused_at_`): a chunk uploaded while paused stays invisible (transparent 1.0 over its stand-in) for the whole pause. Use a steady clock owned by the compositor for the fade (or complete at once when the clock does not advance). Test: paused clock, chunk uploaded, next frame shows the chunk.
  3. **Blend cost.** Blending is now on for every ground and sea draw (two full-screen read-modify-write passes). Either measure it with the existing GPU timestamps (`bench_frame --gpu-timing` or the equivalent) on the RTX 4090 **and** the Radeon iGPU (`ANNO_GPU_ADAPTER`), before/after, at 2560x1440 group 0, enhanced, and put the two numbers in the perf README; or keep opaque pipeline variants and select the blending pipeline only for fading draws (a flag per `Draw`). If the iGPU cost is above 0.3 ms per frame, do the second.
  4. **Stand-in under a fading chunk.** Nothing keeps the stand-in's source chunks for the 150 ms once the real chunk exists (`stand_in_retain` retains only for visible keys not in the cache; other-group chunks are evicted first). Extend the retain to keys in `fading` (the GPU side knows them; pass the set down or keep the fade keys in `FrameRenderer`). Test: the dissolve-over-stand-in test of item 5 with the cache at its eviction limit.
  5. **Tests that are missing:** the dissolve over a *stand-in* (the fade test uses an empty scene: the draw order "stand-in parts, then the real chunk last" and "linear over a stand-in" are pinned by nothing; a mutation dropping the stand-in draw while fading passes), with a value check at mid-fade (expected pixel = lerp within 2 levels), exact at the end; `lookahead_rect` with vx 1001 (ceil), negative vx with positive vy, the height cap; `track_velocity` through `update_chunks`: margin on the leading side, shrinking after the view stops, unchanged speed in the new group's pixels after a group change.
  6. **Velocity spike after a jump of the view** (`track_velocity`): a teleport by D px adds about D / 0.15 px/s whatever dt (a minimap click of 5000 px gives 33,000 px/s, the margin pinned at the cap for 0.45 s, a second of wasted worker time). Reset `vx_`, `vy_`, `last_centre_` when the centre moves more than the view size in one update. Test it.
  7. **Open-sea count per part.** `open_sea_chunks` counts a chunk as covered when *any* part has a source; holes in the cover (`stand_in_cover` drops uncovered parts) show open sea and are not counted. Count the uncovered area (sum of the `dst` rectangles against kChunk²) so the acceptance number means what it says, and re-run the two 1280x800 pan rows; or state the per-chunk granularity in the perf README table header. The first is preferred.
  8. **Memory comment with counted numbers.** The `max_lookahead_chunks` comment says "a few hundred MB"; counted for 2560x1440, three groups, cap 3: keep = 144 chunks standing, about 200-216 on one axis, 297 on a diagonal pan, plus 48 spare, at up to 4.3 MB per land chunk = 0.8 / 1.1 / 1.5 GB worst case (empty sea chunks are small, so real scenes are far below; measure the resident CPU cache in MB on `Single_24.sze` with the pan script and put the number in the comment and the README). Make the cap a viewer option (`--lookahead-chunks`, default 3) so the owner can pick 2 if memory matters; no code change to the bound otherwise.
  9. Nits, one commit: stale "reversing every 90 frames" comments in `bench_frame/main.cpp` (three places; the period is 400 and configurable); `build_ms_` EMA is a non-atomic read-modify-write (CAS loop or a comment that a lost sample is harmless); `adjacent_keys()` doc says "nearest group first" but the sort interleaves groups by distance (fix the doc); a prefetched chunk rebuilt with its flow destroys its old planes instead of pooling them (`it->second = std::move(fresh)` in the prefetch path); `resident` is recounted over all GPU chunks every idle frame (a maintained counter); the `prefetch_chunks` comment's "2 x 7 x 5 = 70" understates a 2560x1440 neighbour view with the margin (about 10 x 6 per group), say so.
- Gates as always; `ctest -j 8` with the game dir is expected to hit the T10 bad_alloc until T10b lands: list the three tests and run them alone, as in the T1d report. Then report and **stop for the owner check** (command below).
- **Owner check command (the T1d worktree, not `main`):** `cd C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T1d\build\msvc\src\app\Release; $g = "C:\Users\altin\anno1503\game\app"; .\anno1503.exe --view "$g\Scenes\Islands\Dschungel\D_256x256_0045_5B.scp" --game-dir $g --water enhanced`. The owner's 2026-10-20 log that still showed artifacts came from `C:\Users\altin\anno1503\build\...` (the `main` build of 20:21, T1c code); the T1d build is from 20:58 in the T1d worktree.

### T12 — CI on `main` is red: two warnings-as-errors in render (since the T1c merge 3c8876b) · `DONE` (PR #2, all ten jobs green, merged as ac94a7b on 2026-10-21; three more errors behind the first one in `bench_frame` fixed too; new CPU test for the stand-in ships flag)
- **Evidence:** every CI run since `bd81096` fails four jobs (`linux-gcc`, `linux-gcc-release`, `linux-clang-asan`, `macos-clang`); `windows-msvc` passes, which is why the local gate did not see it. The orchestrator merged T1c and T1e without the Linux check: a gate error, corrected in §1 point 9 below. Two errors:
  1. `src/render/tests/gpu_test.cpp` (in the T1c test "GPU stand-in with a hole in the nearest group ...", `std::vector<std::uint32_t> out(static_cast<std::size_t>(view.w) * view.h);`): `-Werror=sign-conversion` on GCC and Clang (`view.h` is `int`). Cast both factors (`to_size` exists in the tests). Check the rest of the T1c/T1d/T1e tests for the same pattern: the Windows compiler does not warn on it, so grep for `* view.h` / `* rect.h` and the like.
  2. `src/render/src/frame.cpp`, end of `stand_in_chunk` (`frames.chunk({cover.front().from, ...})->ships`): GCC 13 `-O3 -Werror=null-dereference`. `FrameRenderer::chunk` returns `nullptr` for a key that is not cached, so this is a real missing guard, not only a warning: take the pointer, set `out.ships` from it when it is non-null (the cover's sources are cached by construction, so a null here is a logic error: `assert`-free, just the guard with a comment), and give the function a test with an empty cover if one is possible.
- **Gate for this task and for every render/app task from now on (§1 point 9):** besides the MSVC build, the WSL check `g++-13 -O3 -Werror` with the project's warning flags over **render, app and their tests**, and a Clang build with `-Wsign-conversion -Wnull-dereference` (the asan preset in WSL, or `clang++ -fsyntax-only` with `cmake/AnnoWarnings.cmake`'s flags on the changed files). Report the exact commands. Then push the branch; the orchestrator merges when CI is green on the branch run (open a PR so CI runs on it, do not merge).

### T1f — Chunk build: profile, exact field shortcuts, progressive delivery · items 1-2 `DONE` (merged as ae8b936 on 2026-10-21, see §4) · items 2b, 3, 4 `READY after T11 stage 1 is merged` (they edit `frame.cpp` / `scene.cpp`; see the Order) · branch `task/T1f-chunk-build-speed` (continue on it after merging `main`; worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T1f`)
- **Evidence:** the T1d report measures 100-250 ms per chunk on one worker (a cold frame of 12 chunks 118-247 ms with 16 threads); the perf README of 2026-10-18 measured about 12 ms per 512² chunk on one core, and a river chunk of the canyon island 56-83 ms. Something in between (the river lattice, the enhanced sea per chunk, the apron probes, below-normal thread priority against a busy window thread, memory bandwidth with 16 builders) made chunk building about ten times slower, and no margin can hide a view that crosses the screen in a quarter of a second (the T1d report: a 5000 px/s pan still shows open sea for hundreds of frames).
- **Owner decision 2026-10-20 ("tier 3 now"):** the sprite passes move to the GPU in **T11**; this task keeps what T11 does not touch: the water field, shore distance, terrain and flow passes (orchestrator's main suspect for the ten-fold growth: island properties recomputed per chunk at chunk resolution, `water_field_from` with `blurred_alpha`, `chamfer`, `blend_rivers_into_sea`, and one `terrain_at` call per pixel) and the delivery latency. Items, one commit each: (1) the profile table (below) first, it decides the rest and feeds T11's design; (2) **redefined 2026-10-21 after the profile and the executor's memory finding** (a per-pixel field per island is about 700 MB for the canyon island at group 0: not acceptable, and PLAN 0.1 forbids a design that only works for small islands). The field is expensive only where it can change: the coast band and the river beds. So: per island and zoom group, once, in the background and tiny (tile resolution or a coarse pixel grid, a few KB per island), classify each cell as **deep land**, **deep sea** or **band** by a conservative bound on the spline and noise terms (cells whose every pixel provably gets the trivial value); the chunk builder evaluates the full per-pixel spline and noise only for band cells and writes the trivial values elsewhere. **Pixels identical, digests unchanged** (the bound must be a proof, not a tolerance: write it into `docs/render/iso.md` with the terms it covers). The same classification gives the terrain stage its shortcut (per-tile corner heights rasterised per tile instead of `terrain_at` per pixel, exact). Not option 3 (coarse precompute with interpolation): it changes pixels and would need an owner decision; not per-island storage at any group. Expected: the band is O(coast length × band width), so the field cost no longer scales with the island's area; measure the band share per island and group in the README; (3) progressive delivery: a chunk is handed over in two stages with two serials, ground and fields first, objects later, so land appears first and the T1e dissolve hides the second step; (4) a `kChunk` 256 experiment behind a config value (it is a `constexpr` today: make it a `FrameRenderer` option, then measure latency per chunk, chunks per frame, draw calls and p99 at 2560x1440 for 256 and 512); the owner picks the default from the numbers.
- **Profile first:** one chunk build per stage (`render_chunk` stages: tile cull, object cull, sprite decode, ground, objects, sea field, terrain, flow lattice, apron) on the canyon island and on `Single_24.sze`, groups 0 and 2, one thread and 16 threads, with the existing timing hooks or a `bench_frame --chunk-profile` option; write the table into `docs/perf/README.md` first, then fix the two largest items. Candidates to check, not assumptions: the sea field built per chunk from scratch, sprite decode without the read-write cache hit, the lattice bake per chunk, false sharing or allocation in the builders, builders at below-normal priority losing the CPU to the window thread's spin on a 500 Hz display.
- **Item 2 outcome (2026-10-21):** 50.1 to 29.1 ms (canyon, group 0, one thread), 44.2 to 21.4 ms (`Single_24`); both targets missed (field + terrain 33-57 % of a chunk; the 25 ms not reached). Orchestrator decision: **accepted and closed as it is.** What is left is the sea and coast pixels inside the island grids, whose depth byte needs the exact spline plus noise at every pixel: that is the per-pixel CPU design itself, and T11 section 10.1 replaces it on the GPU. No SIMD work on the band pixels: it would be thrown away with T11 and cannot be bit-exact against the scalar reference without its own proof.
- **Item 2b `DONE` (merged as 92257ae on 2026-10-21, §4).** Follow-up, the first commit of item 3: a CI test of the bounded blur and chamfer (see §4). **Item 2b as specified:** blur and chamfer only within the bounding box of the river sprite pixels plus the kernel radius, the executor's estimate 2-3 ms on river chunks (the worst chunks, up to 21 ms of river stages, are what the owner sees as late chunks). Only with the same kind of proof in `iso.md` (a finite blur kernel is exactly 0 outside the box plus its radius; for the chamfer, show what every consumer does with a distance beyond the box, e.g. a clamp, so the values it reads are identical) and the golden digest; if the chamfer proof does not close in an hour, do the blur alone and say so. Then **the 16-thread rerun** of the item 1 and item 2 tables on an idle machine (not measured yet: a game was running), folded into item 4's 8 against 16 workers measurement.
- Acceptance (revised 2026-10-21 with the profile; item 2 closed below target, see above): the per-stage table in the README before/after; the exact removal of the per-pixel field from the CPU is T11's section 10.1 (a GPU field pass from island data, after the GPU objects pass); item 3 also stops the double build of river chunks (the viewer's workers deliver a river chunk without its lattice and build it again when the flow arrives: deliver the lattice as its own stage, or wait for the flow in the worker when it is a few ms away); item 4 adds one measurement: time to a full cold view (24 chunks at 2560x1440) with 8 against 16 workers, since SMT makes each chunk 1.6-2 times slower at 16 (latency against throughput; the default worker count follows the number); the sprite stages are reported as they are (T11 takes them); the GPU = CPU tests and the digests unchanged; no new limit.

### T3 — Continuous zoom (owner proposal 2026-10-20; replaces T2) · stages 1-2 `DONE` (15a4aaa) · stage 3: design approved, default flip merged (2d15db9), level table, reduce reference and GPU reduce pass merged as c6cb39b (PR #7); **stage 3 `DONE`: merged as a1dd296 (PR #9) after the owner's look ("looks good so far")**; the T3 follow-up `DONE` (merged as 3e9cca2, PR #12; §4, 2026-10-21 p, q, r, t) · branch `task/T3-continuous-zoom` (worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T3`)
- **Owner look 2026-10-21 (PR #9 build, canyon island, RTX 4090):** "something seems wrong when its further zoomed out, it seems very 'Pixelig', as if the terrain is not properly rendered on different zoom stages." Cause and the two fixes: §4, 2026-10-21 m. Fix 1 is the look the owner checks next.
- **Owner, 2026-10-20:** "would it make sense to rework the entire zooming behaviour? for instance, having a continuous zoom with a lower, smoother zooming haptic and speed. with a key to return to default zoom level." Orchestrator's assessment: yes, and it is the right next step after T1e, because (1) the stand-in path already draws any group's chunks at any scale on the GPU, so a continuous factor generalises what exists instead of adding a second mechanism; (2) a slow, eased zoom gives the builders the time a hard 2x step never gives them (a wheel notch of about 10 % over 100-150 ms means a group boundary is crossed several hundred ms after the zoom begins, and the T1e margin has that long to build), so the swap becomes a resolution change at an already-correct scale, not a content change; (3) the animated step (T2) is a special case of it and is dropped. It does **not** remove the open-sea pop-in of a pan onto an unbuilt area: that is T1f.
- **Design (write it into `docs/render/iso.md` before code):** the view has a continuous scale `z` (scene pixels of group 0 per screen pixel, or the tile width in screen pixels); the compositor draws every visible chunk from the nearest built group at `z / scale(group)` (the piece uniform's scale, as stand-ins do), choosing the group whose tile size is closest *above* the current one (downscale, not upscale) when it is built, else the nearest built; switch group at the geometric middle between two tile sizes so a resolution change is least visible, with the T1e dissolve. The wheel changes a target `z` by a configurable factor per notch (default about 1.1, owner-tunable), eased to the target over a configurable time (default about 120 ms, ease-out), anchored at the cursor; keys `+`/`-` do the same in larger steps; a key (`Home` or `0`, owner picks) returns to the default zoom (group 1 scale) with the same easing. Zoom-out beyond group 2 by scaling group 2 down, up to a configurable minimum (default: the whole scene fits the window). The three original zoom levels stay as the content tiers and as an optional *stepped* mode (`--zoom-mode stepped|continuous`), so the original feel is reproducible; the default is the owner's decision (OQ-095).
- **Scalability that must be designed for, measured in the README:** at scale 0.25 of group 2 a 4K view covers roughly 120 chunks of 512 px: chunk textures must be sampled from mip levels (generate mips on upload, or a downsampled tier) or VRAM and bandwidth grow 16x; the visible-chunk set, the CPU cache and the prefetch caps scale with the view in chunks, so every bound must be stated in chunks of the *shown* group and checked at the minimum zoom. Table of visible chunks, resident MB and p50/p95/p99/max per zoom factor at 1280x800 and 2560x1440; no busy frame above 8 ms while zooming from minimum to maximum and back with a pan.
- Sampling: bilinear for scale < 1 from the mip chain, nearest for scale > 1 (the stand-in rule) unless the owner prefers bilinear: produce both as PNG pairs for the owner. GPU = CPU reference tests for the continuous scale (the CPU path draws the same nearest-group rule at the same scale, so headless renders stay the reference); the digests of integer scales unchanged.
- Order inside the task, one commit each with an owner look after 2 and 4: 1. scale in the compositor (no input change, `--zoom-factor` option for the bench and `--png`), 2. the eased wheel and the reset key in the viewer, 3. mips and the zoom-out range, 4. the stepped mode and the default per OQ-095.
- **Stage 3 (2026-10-21, after the review of stages 1-2):** as built, the picture is composed on one group's grid and resampled once (the executor's deviation, accepted). That grid grows with 1 / scale² when zooming out: at scale 0.3 a 2560x1440 window needs an 8533 px grid, already above the 8192 cap, and every pass (sea, fluid, chunks) runs on that grid. So stage 3 is **not** "mips of the composed grid". Condition: **the composed grid stays O(window) at every zoom.** The way that fits the design: derived content tiers below group 2 (the zoom groups are a list, PLAN 0.1), whose chunk planes are the group-2 chunks reduced 2x, 4x, ... on the GPU at upload (box, the same arithmetic as the Box filter) with the sea pattern at that tier, so the compositor keeps its one-scene-pixel-per-grid-pixel rule, the visible chunk count stays bounded in chunks of the shown tier, and no CPU chunk build is added. Write the design into `iso.md` "Continuous zoom" first (a few paragraphs: tier list, what is derived, the cache key, the stand-in and prefetch rules for a derived tier, the minimum zoom = whole scene fits), report it as `REVIEW` before the code. Also in stage 3, each its own commit: (a) the resample pass inside the GPU timestamp pair (`gpu_ms` omits it today); (b) `--zoom-factor` for `bench_frame` and `--png` with a CPU resample of the composed CPU frame using the tests' CPU model of the shader, so headless renders are the reference at a fractional scale: GPU = CPU within 1 per channel on the canyon island and `Single_24` at 0.8 and 1.3, and PNG pairs nearest / bilinear / box of one view for the owner (OQ-095 item 5); (c) the velocity estimate of the margin must not see the zoom's camera move (subtract the anchor term or treat the zoom frame as a jump), tested; (d) the sea pattern of the starting group is built on the window thread in the first frame (28 ms in the owner's log, "sea 27.96"): build it before the window shows or on a worker, so the first frame is not a hitch; (e) the README table the entry asks for: visible chunks, resident MB, p50/p95/p99/max per zoom factor at 1280x800 and 2560x1440, **on the RTX 4090 and on the Radeon iGPU** (the owner's 500 Hz monitor hangs on the iGPU; the Box filter is up to 64 loads per pixel there), zooming from the minimum to the maximum and back with a pan, no busy frame above 8 ms; (f) **owner decision 2026-10-21 (OQ-095 items 1-3): `continuous` is the viewer default** with the values as built (1.1 per notch, 120 ms, `Home` / `0` back to group 1's width); `--zoom-mode stepped` stays the faithful option. Do this as the first commit of stage 3 (it is small and the owner uses the build): flip the default in `ZoomOptions`, print the "needs the GPU renderer; zooming in steps" line only when `--zoom-mode continuous` was given explicitly (with the CPU renderer the default must fall back silently), update the usage text, `iso.md` and the CLI tests. Items 4 and 5 of OQ-095 (minimum zoom, nearest vs bilinear) follow the stage 3 design note and the PNG pairs. **Two more owner decisions of 2026-10-21 for stage 3, each its own commit after (a)-(e):** (g) **OQ-076:** a 250 ms cross-fade from the analytic coast waves into the simulated wave window after a group switch (today the coast shows analytic waves for 0.1-0.4 s; with the continuous zoom switches are frequent): the compositor blends the two wave sources by a per-group arrival time on the fade clock, the CPU reference does the same, test at 1/4 and 1/2 of the fade as the T1e dissolve is tested; measure the blend's cost in the README; (h) **OQ-093:** `GpuLimits::prewarm_chunks` derived from the view (visible chunks of the largest group that can be shown x planes per chunk, plus the spare margin) and bounded by a VRAM budget field with its reason, instead of the fixed 24; the number is recomputed on a window resize; report the MB at 1280x800, 2560x1440 and 3840x2160. Also closed by the owner 2026-10-21 without work: OQ-077 (stand-ins confirmed), OQ-091 (leave), OQ-092 (resolved by T1e + T3), OQ-078 / OQ-083 (river flow and light as they are).

### T11 — GPU-built chunks: the sprite passes on the GPU (owner decision 2026-10-20, "tier 3 now") · stages 0-1 `DONE` (stage 1 merged as daaa849 on 2026-10-21, §4) · **stage 2a merged as 9f54fec (PR #17, 2026-10-22 f); `OWNER-CHECK`** (the viewer check), then the per-chunk dispatch timestamps (correction 2026-10-23 j: this header still said `IN PROGRESS`) · was: PR #17, reviewed 2026-10-22 c. The compute pass is bit-exact on both adapters. Open: derived levels, CPU build on atlas overflow, the pipeline created at start, the B4 measurements, the viewer check, stand-in tests with both builders, mutation evidence, G1–G4. Then OWNER-CHECK. · branch `task/T11-gpu-chunks` (continue after merging `main`; worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T11`)
- **Goal:** a chunk's ground and objects pixels are no longer composited on the CPU. The CPU produces a *draw list* per chunk (sprite frame id, position, depth order, colour table, alpha level, shadow flag, lift: a few thousand instances, microseconds), and the GPU renders the list into the chunk's ground and objects planes in a render pass from sprite atlases. Everything downstream (chunk cache keyed by `ChunkKey`, compositor, stand-ins, prefetch, dissolve) stays as it is: a chunk is still "textures on the GPU", only their producer changes. Why now (owner): chunk builds of 100-250 ms are the limit behind every pop-in; per-sprite work belongs on the GPU (CLAUDE.md); and the sim will move thousands of sprites per tick (carriers, civilians, animals, ships), which static chunks cannot hold, so one GPU sprite path serves both the static world and the moving one instead of two paths.
- **What exists and must be reused, not duplicated:** the compositor already has a growable sprite atlas (`GpuLimits::atlas_size` 2048, `max_atlas_layers`, `Impl::Atlas` in `gpu.cpp`), a sprite pipeline (`sprite_vs` / `sprite_fs` in `gpu.wgsl`) and per-frame instanced sprite draws for ships and soldiers above and below the waterline (`Single_24` draws 396 sprites, within 1 per channel of the CPU path, atlas growth tested). T11 generalises that path to the chunk content. The CPU blitter (`iso.cpp`, `docs/render/iso.md` "Blitting", `docs/re/annodisplay.md`: signed `z` depth offsets, alpha levels, 5/8 shadows that compound, `H`/`V`/`M`/`W` brushes, `BSH_RGBWZ` column expansion and the 50 % checkerboard, no width clipping, signed `i16` anchors, player colour tables, per-corner elevation) stays the reference and the oracle; whatever it does the shader does, pinned by tests.
- **Stage 0, design, written into `docs/render/gpu-chunks.md` before any code, reviewed by the orchestrator (report it as a `REVIEW` of its own):** (a) the draw list format (one fixed-width record per instance; what the ground pass needs: tile frames with per-corner heights; what the objects pass needs: painter's order key, shadow before sprite, alpha level, colour table index, water/above-water split as today); (b) atlas residency: all 28,518 building and 725,424 figure frames cannot be resident (count the bytes per group from `docs/render/iso.md` "Fidelity": about 78 M covered pixels for buildings at group 0 alone), so residency is per scene and lazy, like a glyph cache: frames referenced by the draw lists of the chunks in `keep` are resident, with an LRU and a configurable budget in MB (default from the adapter's limits, reason in the comment); a chunk whose frames are not all resident yet is built after their upload, never with holes; measure how many frames a scenario references per group (`Single_24` as the largest) and put the numbers into the design; (c) the render pass: render-to-texture into the chunk's ground and objects planes (and the `base` plane), with the painter's order realised by draw order (one instanced draw per chunk per layer, instances sorted on the CPU), shadows as a blend state or a second pass, alpha levels by instance; where the compositor needs the `terrain` and `field` planes those still come from the CPU (T1f item 2) until a later stage moves them; (d) scheduling: chunk GPU work goes into the same frame's command encoder under a budget (`GpuLimits`, chunks per frame and ms, reason stated); the window thread never waits; the CPU draw-list build runs on the existing `ChunkWorkers`; (e) the CPU reference remains: the `--png` path, the digests and `anno-dump` stay the CPU compositing; a GPU-built chunk equals the CPU chunk within 1 per channel on every plane, tested per group on `Single_01`, `Single_24` and the canyon island, plus a synthetic scene that exercises shadows over translucent sprites, the checkerboard, compounding shadows and negative anchors (the mutation survivors of the earlier compositor review); (f) the fallback: without a GPU (headless, CI, the CPU path) chunks are built as today; the switch is a `FrameRenderer` option, default GPU in the viewer once the owner accepted it; (g) limits: atlas size and layers, residency budget, chunks per frame are config with reasons; no fixed arrays, no narrowing of ids (PLAN 0.1); (h) what moves later: the sea field and terrain planes (compute shader from island data), moving units from the sim (the same draw-list records, per frame instead of per chunk), so the design names those extensions without building them.
- **Stage 1:** atlas residency and the draw-list builder on the CPU, with a test that the draw list of a chunk reproduces the CPU chunk when replayed through the CPU blitter (so the list is proven before any shader work). **Stage 2:** the GPU render pass for the ground and objects planes, GPU = CPU tests, `bench_frame --chunks gpu` with the per-stage numbers, then the **owner check** on the canyon island (pan fast, zoom with the wheel) and on `Single_24.sze`. **Stage 3:** the cache and prefetch paths take GPU-built chunks (the `serial` rule unchanged), stand-ins source from them, measurements of a 5000 px/s pan (the case T1d could not cover) and of the cold frame; `docs/perf/README.md` table: chunk latency on the CPU path against the GPU path per group, resident atlas MB, frame p50/p95/p99/max at 1280x800 and 2560x1440 with the pan-and-zoom script, on the RTX 4090 and on the Radeon iGPU.
- **Late-game stress fixture (owner question 2026-10-21, "does it hold in a large late-game multiplayer match"):** stage 3 adds a synthetic scene generator (`fixtures/` code, no original files committed; the scene is built at test time from the game dir): every island of `Single_24.sze` covered with dense city blocks (tens of thousands of buildings, every building type present), thousands of soldiers and ships, so the sprite count per chunk is several times the original scenarios' maximum of 3,608. `bench_frame` runs it at 2560x1440 with the pan-and-zoom script, CPU builder against GPU builder: chunk latency, frame p50/p95/p99/max, resident atlas MiB, on both GPUs; the perf README states the per-chunk GPU time as a function of overdraw (not of instance count). The acceptance below must hold on that scene too.
- Acceptance: a visible chunk is on screen within 20 ms of its request on the 4090 at 2560x1440 (list build + atlas upload of missing frames + the pass), within 50 ms on the iGPU; no busy frame above 8 ms; `open_sea_chunks` 0 at the 5000 px/s pan; GPU = CPU within 1 per channel on all three scenes and the synthetic one; the CPU path untouched in output; no new fixed limit.

    2. **Session start (owner says "go")** as in T15.B2 step 2; `camera()` and `play.check_addresses`; `quicksave` (F10) right after the scenario is up: this is the restore point for every macro trial (`quickload` = F9, then 3 s).
    3. **`place(be, key, x, y, rotation=0, align=False)` in `play.py`:** open the menu (`B`), click the category (`screens.json` `category_column_y[key's category]`), click the grid cell (`grid_cells.pioneers[category][key]` → `grid.col_x`/`row_y`), press `.` `rotation` times, `goto_tile(x, y)`, hold Ctrl when `align`, click, Esc. **Verify** (both, in this order): (a) `tile(island, x, y).type` equals the type's id (for a footprint larger than 1×1 check every footprint tile has it; the footprint comes from `Anlagen.dat` HEAD[0]/[1] via `tools/anlagen_roads.py`'s parser, swapped for odd rotations); (b) the player's money, tools and wood fell by the type's cost within the bounds `opening.py` uses (`placement_ok`). Return the verdict and the reason on failure (`not unlocked`: the cell is a `?`; `outside`: the game refused — no tile change; `no funds`; `blocked`). Types for this task: pioneer house (`public` [1,1]), dirt road (`roads` [1,1]), forester (`farms` [1,2]), weaver / tannery (`workshops` [1,1] — say which it is from the info panel's name), market (`public` [2,4]); their ids and costs from the files (`anlagen_roads.py`, `opening.json`).
    4. **`road(be, path)`:** a list of tiles; drag from the first to the last of each straight segment in placement mode of the dirt road (the drag primitive of `control.py`), verify every tile's type id is a road type (`2525..2528`, `tools/anlagen_roads.py` table) and the gold fell by 5 per new tile. **`demolish(be, x, y)`:** the demolish tool (`ui-and-placement.md`/the manual: the right panel's demolish button or the `D` menu — find it on screen once, record its fraction in `screens.json`), `goto_tile`, click; verify the tile's type id returns to the terrain value it had before (read it before placing) and the refund if any (record the observed delta; no refund rule is assumed).
    5. **Ten trials each from the quick save:** `quickload`, then `place` of a house, a forester, a workshop and a market on tiles chosen around the Kontor (inside the yellow area; the island reader tells land from water: type id of empty land vs water — record the two values seen), `road` of a 6-tile L-path to the house, `demolish` of the house; each trial logged with the verdict; a failure's reason named. Then one deliberate failure of each kind (a house in the water; a road through the Kontor; a `?` cell) to show the reasons are right.
    6. **Close and report.** `control.py close`; REPORTS T15.C with the trial table (10 × 4 places, 10 roads, 10 demolishes, the deliberate failures), the island reader's values (land/water type ids seen, the Kontor's), the `screens.json` additions (demolish button, the rotate and align behaviour); `play-original.md` §6 stage C row ticked or its misses; `ui-and-placement.md` §3 corrected where the flow differed. Acceptance: (a) the readers with their tests, (b) `place`/`road`/`demolish` each verified 10× from the quick save with failure reasons named, (c) the deliberate failures classified, (d) hashes unchanged at close, (e) nothing of the original in git; PR, not merged. No `src/` change, no C++ build.

- **Owner, 2026-10-21:** "Once the current tasks at the executor are done and approved, let us fix the open points under M1 and M2. Now that i am on my Windows machine, this should be possible. I also want you to instruct the executor, to start the game or simply the renderer (AND the original game in order to compare to the original, when necessary and appropriate, for instance for M2) and simulate it through all necessary parameters or scenarios."
- **Why it is possible now:** this PC is x86-64 Windows 11, and `game/app` is a complete GOG 2.0.0.5 install (`1503Startup.exe`, the game DLLs, `goggame-*`), so the 32-bit original can run here. Everything in PLAN M1/M2 and `docs/testing/checklist.md` that waited for "an x86 Windows machine" (fixtures F1-F9, the M2.1 savegame corpus) and a pixel comparison of our renderer with the original become possible.
- **Rules for running the original (every stage):** (a) **never run it from `game/app`** (read-only; the game writes profiles, settings and saves into its folder): copy it once to `C:\anno1503-original\` (outside the repo) and run that copy; record SHA-256 of the copy's executables and DLLs against `game/app`. (b) **Nothing derived from the original's output goes into git:** screenshots, savegames, memory dumps and recordings live in `cache/orig/<session>/` or `saves/` (both git-ignored); commit scripts, protocols, numbers, hashes and findings. (c) **Driving the desktop** (the game takes focus, keyboard and mouse, maybe the whole screen) **only in a session the owner has agreed to**: say beforehand what will run, for how long and how to stop it (a hotkey, or closing the process); never while the owner works; prefer methods that work in a window and in the background. (d) **Third-party tools** (for example a DirectDraw wrapper for a windowed mode such as dgVoodoo2 or cnc-ddraw, Frida, x32dbg, pywinauto, mss): each listed with version, licence and reason in the stage 0 design and installed only after the owner agrees; nothing of them in git; Python packages via `--target` under the scratchpad, scripts run with `-I`. (e) Observation is black-box and by memory reading of the owner's licensed copy; findings go into `docs/re/` with module and address as before; nothing of the original's code or data is copied into `src/`.
- **Stage 0, design and smoke run (`REVIEW` before more code):** (1) does the copy start (`1503Startup.exe`), with which renderer and resolution, can it run windowed (game option, registry, or a wrapper), what breaks on this PC (two GPUs, the 500 Hz monitor on the iGPU); exact steps. (2) Control by script: start a given scenario (command line or menu navigation by input injection, each step confirmed by a screenshot), pause, game speed, move the camera to a world position and a zoom group, save and load. (3) Observation: screenshots of the game's window with a method that works with its renderer; state from memory: module bases (enumerate the modules; the 2006 DLLs probably load at their preferred bases: check), the globals the RE notes name (world clock, PRNG cursor, game speed, gold, stocks, camera position, zoom group), read by `ReadProcessMemory` from a script, or hooks at the tick function for per-tick logs; which method and why. (4) The harness in `tools/orig/` (Python, `-I`): launch, goto, shot, peek, save; a protocol file for scripted sessions; outputs and a timestamped log in `cache/orig/<session>/`. (5) What our side needs to show the same view and state: `--png` at a camera position and zoom group in faithful water, the sim's fixtures as tests. Report the design and the smoke result: one screenshot of the original taken by the script, and one memory value read and checked against what the game shows.
- **Stage 1, the M2 fixtures against the running original:** F1-F9 of `docs/re/tick-loop.md` (game clock, speeds 50-400 %, step sizes, the PRNG cursor and the F6 values; the multiplayer ones if two instances can run on this PC), and the fixture specs of `production.md` (the 500-tick step), `population.md` (houses every 2,500 ticks, taxes every 2,000), `fire-and-ships.md`. Each measured in the original; the RE note gets "verified by running" with date and method; a mismatch becomes an OQ and, where `src/sim` implements the subsystem, a fix with a test. Where the sim implements it, the same inputs through `src/sim` and compared: the start of `anno-diff`.
- **Stage 2, the M2.1 savegame corpus by script** (PLAN M2.1's protocol: a baseline, N idle minutes, single-action pairs, a mid-game save, an add-on save, multiplayer if it runs locally); OQ-020 (where and in what format the game saves); `anno-diff save <a> <b>` in C++ (changed chunks, then byte ranges); `docs/re/savegames.md` (file names, sizes, SHA-256, what was done, the values read from memory and from the screen); the changed fields cross-referenced with the `SaveGame` code paths.
- **Stage 3, the renderer against the original (M1.6):** for the ten `0200Single` scenarios and the canyon island, zoom groups 0-2, several camera positions each (coast, river, town, mountains, ships): the original's screenshot (paused, game time noted) against our `--png` in faithful water at the same camera and zoom; per view the share of pixels equal, within 1, within 8, and a difference heat map (in `cache/`); animation phases aligned or masked. The findings settle OQ-026 (projection, draw order), OQ-024 (`GFXID`), OQ-023 (figure frames), player colours and the M1.6 open list (people and soldiers, animations, terrain lighting): each difference is fixed (bug) or becomes an OQ (look). Enhanced water is not compared (not original). The owner gets a side-by-side page of the worst views (local, not published).
- **Stage 4, the remaining M1 format points:** the `Texte.dat` join and `anno-dump anlagen` with names (static: may be the first commit, while stage 0 waits for its review); the `Anlagen.dat` gameplay records (OQ-015: `HEAD`, `PRODTYPE`, `PROD3`, `WARE`, `ROHSTOFF`, the masks, `INFRAMIN`, `OBJECTPOS`, `BAUSAMPLE`, `ENTRANCE`) from the RE plus experiments in the copy (a value changed in a copied data or scenario file, the effect observed; never in `game/`); the other `SCENE` records (`HEAD`, `MAIN`, ...), the other island records (OQ-024), the `.scp` trailer (OQ-001), versioned chunk names in the loader (M1.5), plain-text field meanings (OQ-029), figure record semantics (OQ-023), `FileGame.dll` symbols (OQ-011), the interface vtables (`docs/re/interfaces.md`). The Amsel cross-check needs the owner (a contact): list it as a question. Update PLAN M1/M2 (several boxes are stale: the Ghidra project and the static analysis are done) and the checklist.
- **Acceptance:** every open M1/M2 point is closed with evidence or is a precise OQ that says what remains; the harness is reusable for M3's differential tests; nothing of the original in git; the owner agreed to every desktop session and every third-party tool.

### T3 (old entry) — Smooth zoom, step 3 · superseded by the T3 entry above (kept for the record)
- A continuous zoom factor on the GPU from the nearest built group (bilinear or area-averaging for scale < 1, nearest or bilinear for > 1; measure both; the owner picks the look). Zoom-out beyond group 2 by scaling group 2 further down. **Scalability warning that must be designed for:** at scale 0.25 of group 2 a 4K view covers roughly 120 chunks of 512 px; chunk textures must then be sampled from mip levels (generate mips on upload, or a downsampled chunk tier), otherwise VRAM and bandwidth grow 16×. Produce a table of visible chunks, VRAM and frame time per zoom factor in `docs/perf/README.md`. Min/max zoom are config values with defaults.

### T4 — Smooth zoom, step 4: prebuild adjacent groups in the background · done inside T1d/T1e (margin and neighbour-group prefetch); the `Single_24.sze` measurement is in the T1d perf section
- Keep the adjacent groups' chunks of the visible area warm (priority below the current group's, bounded memory, evicted first), so the zoom cost does not depend on the game load. Measure: zoom script with a full 8-player scene (`Single_24.sze`) must show no chunk-receive lines in the first second after a step for areas that were visible before the step.
