### T6c — Gap patch that deepens only real gap cells (OQ-068, owner decision 2026-10-20: option 3) · `READY` · `PARALLEL-OK` (not at the same time as T5: both touch `src/render/src/sea.cpp` / `river*`) · branch `task/T6c-gap-patch-cells` from `main`

### T10 — Reproduce the digest-test flake under load · `REVIEW` → T10b (reviewed 2026-10-20: do not merge as is)
- **Finding (T0 report):** in one full `ctest -j 16` run with `ANNO_TEST_THREADS=16`, `anno_render_tests.scene renders match the recorded digests …` failed; it passed alone and in two more full runs. The output was not kept (the CTest logs rotate; `LastTestsFailed.log` of 13:15 names it, nothing else survives). A digest test that fails under load is either memory exhaustion (31 GB RAM; that test alone renders up to 16 whole scenes of about 640 MB each while 15 other tests run) or a data race in the parallel scene renderer. The second would break determinism, so this is not allowed to stay unexplained.
- **Do:** (1) run that test alone 3× with `ANNO_TEST_THREADS=16` while a parallel full ctest runs, with output captured (`ctest --preset msvc -R "recorded digests" --output-on-failure -O <scratch>/run<n>.log`), and note peak commit charge (Task Manager or `Get-Counter '\Memory\Committed Bytes'`); (2) if a failure shows a digest mismatch (not a crash or `bad_alloc`): report `BLOCKED` with the log at once, do not fix; (3) if it is memory: make the per-test thread cap in `src/test_support/include/anno/test/parallel.hpp` also bounded by free physical memory (threads ≤ free RAM / 1 GB, reason in a comment, env override kept) and add a CTest `PROCESSORS`/`RESOURCE_GROUPS` or memory property so the heavy corpus tests do not all run at once; (4) in any case make CTest keep the failing output: `ctest` with `--output-log` in the documented command (`CLAUDE.md` build line and the memory note's command) so a failure is never lost again.
- **Acceptance:** either a captured mismatch (then stop) or a fix with three green full runs at `-j 16` and the peak commit charge before/after in the report.

### T7b — S8 doc fixes and the wire-version decision · `DONE` (merged with S8 as d352eb8, PR #14, 2026-10-22; §4) · `PARALLEL-OK` · branch `task/T7-sim-s8-commands` · small
- **Review verdict on ed59370:** all nine S8 tests exist with their meaning; the exit criterion is enforced by `static_assert`; the decoder is total and canonical on hostile input (length and count lies, truncation, 65,536 types, 100,000 random records, 7-byte turn entries); apply is validation-only with the hash property tested over 3,000 random commands; no float, no unordered container, no draw outside `draw.hpp`, no new state field, goldens unchanged; GCC 13 -O3 and ASan/UBSan green locally. Mergeable after:
  1. `docs/PLAN.md:221` still says S0–S7 and `:266` "continue S8–S15": update to S0–S8. `docs/sim/state.md:37` and the S8 paragraph say 23 new cases / 426; the files hold 24 and the binary lists 427 (the last commit added one): correct.
  2. **Orchestrator decision, write it into state.md §8.5:** `kCommandWireVersion` is **not** carried per record. It is carried once in the S9 replay-log header and once in the M4 lockstep handshake (together with the content hash, PLAN 0.1); a peer whose version differs is refused at the handshake. Rationale: records are canonical and length-framed, a per-record version buys nothing once the session has agreed, and it would cost two bytes per command on the wire. Also note in §8.5 that S9 adds a `record_size(span)` / `decode_turn(span, vector<TurnEntry>&)` framing helper so header parsing lives in one place.
  3. Fold `island_at_mut` (`apply.cpp:21-28`) and `World::island_at` (`sim.cpp:154-161`) into one lookup with a const and a non-const overload (O(islands) is acceptable for S8; two copies are not). Note in state.md §4.14 that a derived island index replaces the scan when the island count grows (perf backlog).
  4. Carry over to T8 (S9) as requirements: the framing helper above; the net layer drops a peer after N decode errors (M4 note); an AI-issued `SetSpeed` inside `advance` is allowed by spec §8.2 item 4 and stays so (the seam is trusted code, not a rules question).
- Then gates (sim: MSVC, GCC -O3, ASan in WSL as before), report, and the orchestrator merges.

### T10b — Test memory: make the mitigation visible and schedule the heavy tests apart · **`DONE`** (merged as 36d7405 on 2026-10-23, PR #24, CI green; §4) · was `REVIEW` → approved 2026-10-23 i · `PARALLEL-OK` · branch `task/T10-digest-flake`
- **Added 2026-10-21 (from T12):** (a) commit the executor's warning check as `tools/check_unix_warnings.sh` (GCC 13 and Clang 18 in WSL with the CI flags over every `.cpp` under `src/`, deps from `build/msvc/_deps`, the viewer and GPU defines on), with a line in `CLAUDE.md`'s environment notes; it is the gate of §1 point 9; (b) `anno_dump_agrees_with_python` joined the `-j 16` `bad_alloc` list: cover it the same way as the render tests; (c) tiny: the three private fields of `viewer.cpp` unused without `ANNO_VIEWER_GPU` (`-Wunused-private-field` on Clang): guard them with the same `#ifdef` as their uses.
- **Review verdict on 9f42def:** keep the memory bound in `test_threads()`, `memory.cpp` (Windows min(AvailPhys, AvailPageFile) is the right quantity), the preset output log and the CLAUDE.md line; retry results are written by index so output equals the serial run; CI is unaffected (the game-dir tests skip there). Not mergeable because the wait and the retry are silent and the wait has a dead tail. Fix:
  1. **HIGH: silent mitigation.** Neither a memory wait (up to 120 s per item) nor a bad_alloc retry prints anything, so "no bad_alloc occurred in the three green runs" is unsupported and a slow run cannot be explained from the log this branch adds. Count waits and retries, print one stderr line per retry and one summary line at the end of `parallel_indexed`.
  2. **MEDIUM: dead tail.** `wait_for_memory` runs before `next++` even when all items are taken, so after the last item each remaining worker can poll up to 120 s before it sees `i >= n`, and the join waits for them. Check `next >= n` before waiting.
  3. **MEDIUM: masking.** A retried bad_alloc re-runs the item silently; a race that corrupts a size throws bad_alloc, not bad_array_new_length, and would be hidden. Add `ANNO_TEST_BAD_ALLOC_RETRIES` (default 5, 0 = off) and keep the per-retry log line from item 1.
  4. **The actual scheduling fix.** The reviewer checked the vendored `doctest.cmake:125-130`: `doctest_discover_tests` hashes the test spec into the generated file names, so it can be called twice on one executable. Register the heavy corpus cases of `anno_render_tests` (the three that render whole scenes: "scene renders match the recorded digests", "legacy river shading reproduces the previous enhanced digests", "the light of the flow shading against the legacy shimmer", plus any other case that calls `parallel_indexed` over whole scenes) with `TEST_SPEC -tc=<patterns>` and `PROPERTIES PROCESSORS <n>` (or a `RESOURCE_LOCK heavy_render`), and the rest with `-tce=<same patterns>`. At `-j 16` ctest then runs a heavy case alone, deterministically, which removes the overload (3 heavy tests × 16 render threads + 13 other jobs) at the scheduler instead of reacting to it. `PROCESSORS` follows the machine (`ProcessorCount`), not a literal. Verify: three full `ctest -j 16` runs with `ANNO_TEST_THREADS=16`, peak commit charge sampled, no wait or retry line in the log.
  5. LOW: a free value of 0 is treated as "unknown" and switches the bound off; on Windows `AvailPageFile == 0` is exactly the bad_alloc condition: return an optional for a failed query and keep 0 as 0. Clamp `mb * 1024 * 1024` and `memory_wait_seconds() * 4` against wrap.
- Then gates (test_support: MSVC and GCC -O3), report, and the orchestrator merges.

Consequence stated in `state.md` §10 and OQ-new: for a given seed the rebuild picks a different island than the original would (the original's own pick is not reproducible across installs either, so no differential fixture can compare it). The owner confirms or changes the default; the work does not wait.

The resolved template is loaded by the caller (`formats::insel` on the `.scp`), turned into an `IslandSpec` by the same function stage 1 uses for `INSEL5` (pull the island-building code of `load_scenario` into `island_from_insel(const insel::Island&, slot, x0, y0, …)`), placed at the link's position with the link's number as slot; the assigned ores go onto the island's mountain objects as the original does it (D: which objects, which draw, which amount → `DepositSpec` with `goods` from the ore kind: the 9 `ERZE` kinds → goods ids, O-37, from the table the reader uses; if the goods mapping cannot be read, the deposits become a `LinkOresUnplaced` warning with the counts). ### T19 — The script engine: `&&`/`||` precedence and statements without `;` (OQ-120, OQ-121) · **`DONE`** (merged as d6d5e85 on 2026-10-23, PR #23, CI green) · approved 2026-10-22 i (PR #23; both closed: equal rank grouped to the right, `&&` short-circuits; a missing `;` reads on to the next `;`, one shipped action lost) · `PARALLEL-OK` (docs only: `docs/re/annoscene.md`, `docs/formats/bigbrother.md` §3.4, `docs/open-questions.md`) · branch `task/T19-script-engine-re` from `main`
- **Why:** 28 of the 308 shipped conditions mix `&&` and `||` without parentheses, and four shipped statements have no `;` (`bigbrother.md` §3.4). What these missions test, and whether an action is lost, depends on the interpreter. The C++ port of the script engine needs both answered.
- **Do** (static analysis with `tools/disasm.py` and capstone, as in `docs/re/annoscene.md`): find the condition parser and the statement reader in `AnnoScene.dll` (the ops range `0x10011ed0`–`0x10013dab`, O-16; the delimiter table `0x1003d888`–`0x1003d8a0`; the strings "Kein THEN bei Bedingung", "Unbekannter Vergleichsoperator!"); state how a condition with mixed connectives is grouped and evaluated (left to right, `&&` binds tighter, or other), and what the reader does at a missing `;` (ends the statement at `)`/value, or reads on into the next).
- **Acceptance:** both questions answered with module + address for every step, or narrowed with the reason; the 28 mixed conditions and the four statements re-read under the answer (which of them change meaning against a naive reading, by file, short excerpts only); `tools/bigbrother.py` grows a `describe` of the grouping only if the answer is certain; OQ-120/121 updated (closed or narrowed); report and PR.

Nothing of M3's acceptance (S15: the 1-hour run on shipped scenarios) means much on empty islands. The reading already exists in part: `docs/re/production.md` ("Savegame city `STADT2` (`0x1002e170`)": `WARE` 8-byte entries → stock = tons << 8, `HEAD2` u16 at 0x24 = capacity in tons with 0 → 50, `BAUMAT`, `VK`/`EK`, `PROD/ENTRY` = 4-byte position + the building's own record, confirmed), and `state.md` §10 step 4 / O-58 (`0x100264a0` calls `Create(rec, -1, 0)` then the class `Load`).

### T27b — The registry refuses a world whose layout would classify a type differently (owner decision 2026-10-24 w) · `READY` (round w; **the executor itself**, before T30) · `PARALLEL-OK` with T29 (T27b: `types.hpp`/`types.cpp`, `types_test.cpp`, the one test setup named below, `state.md` §5.9, OQ-155; T29: `transport.*`, `transport_test.cpp`, `scenario_test.cpp`) · branch `task/T27b-layout-check` from `main` at 0972e5c or later · worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T27b` · no new OQ number
- **Why:** `BuildingType::cls` (hashed) and `walk_class` (not hashed) are derived from the `ListLayout` the registry was built with; a world may run with another layout (`validate_against` is called with the world's config, a registry is shared between worlds), and then the transport search and the class lookups disagree with the world's lists. T27's fix-up tried a whole-layout digest compare; 10 cases failed, 7 of them only for the schedule, lookup order or roles, which do not enter `list_for` (REPORTS T27 "Fix-up (round v)" item 6).
- **Steps (in order; §1 item 7):**
  1. **Set up.** `git worktree add /c/Users/altin/anno1503-T27b -b task/T27b-layout-check origin/main`; build; `ctest --preset msvc -j 16 -R anno_sim` with the game dir green (the count into REPORTS.md). Builds and ctest **in the foreground**, one per call.
  2. **The check.** In `check_against_config` (`types.cpp`), for every type: recompute what `finish` and `type_from_entry` derive from the layout, under `cfg.layout`: the class (`class_for_prodtype(t.prodtype, cfg.layout).value_or(RuntimeClass::Plain)`, only for types whose class came from that function: read `type_from_entry` and the Builder's `add_*` to see which) and the walk class (the `finish` rule); a mismatch refuses with "type N: the configuration's ListLayout gives class/walk class X, the registry was built with Y". A registry without types accepts any layout. No member is added to `TypeRegistry`.
  3. **Tests.** New case in `types_test.cpp`, "registry refuses a config whose layout reclassifies a type": a registry with one type of PRODTYPE 1 and one of 97 under `ListLayout::original()`; a config whose `block_to_list` drops block 3 (97 loses its list) is refused naming type 97; a config that only changes `schedule` and `roles` is accepted. Mutants: the walk-class comparison removed → the case fails; the class comparison removed → name which case fails or add a sub-case that does.
  4. **The existing cases.** Run the sim suite. By reading the tests only "building scheduler visits the lists in the order of the schedule" (`scheduler_test.cpp`) should fail: its world uses `block_size = 1`, `block_to_list = {4, 5}`, under which the kit's other types have no list. Fix that case's setup so the registry is built with the world's config (the Builder derives the class with its config's layout); change nothing it asserts. If other cases fail, stop and list them (§1 item 7).
  5. **Docs.** `state.md` §5.9 (one sentence: the walk class and the class follow the registry's build layout; `validate_against` refuses a world whose layout would derive other values); OQ-155: a status note "the `HEAD2` trade-list slot counts (39 shipped cities with counts other than 4/6) are not kept; a field comes with the trade stage (owner, 2026-10-24 w)"; REPORTS.md with the acceptance ticked; PR. No `TASKS.md` edit.
- **Acceptance:** (1) the new case and the two mutants; (2) only the named test setup changed, its asserts untouched; (3) every hash unchanged (the full suite with and without the game dir, `sim_stream.py --check`, `scenario_corpus.txt` and `goldens.txt` unchanged); (4) gates of §1 item 4 through the heavy-job lock, in the foreground; (5) PR, not merged.

The Wine runner (`docs/testing/original-wine.md`) is pre-authorized for silent runs; the heavy-job lock applies to a Wine run as to anything else, foreground, one per call.
  2. **The vtable method `[edx+0xec]` on `[this+0x858]` (OQ-175, the next address of T30 step 2).** Find the constructor that installs the vtable with slot `+0xec` matching this call site: start from `0x1000ebb0` (which installs vtables `0x100c18b4` and `0x100c18b0` and reads `[eax+0x858]` from an argument), read its callers to see which engine class supplies the `[this+0x858]` object, then dump that engine's vtable and disassemble its `+0xec` slot instruction by instruction. The method is called with `(engine+0x85c, 0, &buffer, 8)` where `buffer` is 4 dwords (two 8-byte spans from the primary layer block and two from the secondary); the question is how those 16 bytes become a yes/no. Record every D with its address; what is likely gets a note; what is open gets the next address. Update `transport.md` §3.3 and OQ-175.
  3. **The `+0x434` secondary table writer (OQ-175, cont.).** `[this+0x430]` is set by `0x1000dd20` (D, T30 §3.1); `[this+0x434]` reads as a pointer but has no obvious setter in the sweep. Look for the writer by (a) tracking writes to `[reg + k]` where `k` could be `0x434`, (b) checking the constructor at `0x1000ebb0` and its callers (the engine's initial state might set both pointers at once from a struct), (c) `xref.py` on each of the five layer-block start addresses (`0x100d1140 + 0x1c00·L` for L in 0..4) looking for arithmetic that would produce the pointer. State what the secondary points to and what layer / mode (walk, shot) it carries. Open: the one with the clearest D-chain is the answer; if none reach D, keep as the next address under OQ-175.
  4. **Step 7(a): memory compare after a scenario loads (does not need the figure reader).** In the Wine runner (`agent.py` `read`), capture the five 7168-byte blocks `0x100d1140 + 0x1c00·L` (L = 0..4) right after a scenario loads AND after 10 game minutes; compare them byte-wise with the DLL image on disk. Record: "equal", or (if different) the count and offsets of the differing bytes — **no block content in git**. This closes the "written at run time" question T30 left as "D static but open dynamic". Add the Wine automation to `tools/orig/`: a short script that drives the agent to load one scenario (reuse `tools/orig/opening.py` or just `agent.py launch` + screen-wait), then calls `read` on the module+RVAs, then diffs; the diff output goes to `cache/orig/` as the harness already does.
  5. **The figure-position reader in `tools/orig/gamestate.py`** (owner's scope rule, round w: this task names it as a new tool). The current reader covers world, players and city stocks. The figure reader must: find the figure table off the world object (`world + ?` — the sim's own figure table lives at `world + 0x114` per `transport.md` §5.9, but the ORIGINAL's offset is open — one of T30b step 5's sub-questions); decode each figure record enough to export `(id, type, position, tile, state_step, layer)` in a stable JSON; be read-only. Named test cases of the reader: (a) with no scenario loaded, zero figures; (b) after `opening.py` runs, every figure the opening spawned is in the list (counts match the manual's spawn table); (c) its tile at tick N equals its tile at tick N+1 ± a few. **The reader does not touch sim state** (it is a Wine-only harness tool).
  6. **Step 7(b): read the opening's tile records and apply the proposed rule** (`state.md` §5.9's "Proposed rule for T33"). For each of the opening's buildings (market hall, foresters, weaving huts, sheep farms, tannery, hunting lodges), read the tile records of its footprint and the surrounding road tiles, and apply the proposed rule for layer 3 (the cart) and layer 0 (the carrier). Numbers go into REPORTS; a contradiction with step 7(c) is a finding, not something to explain away.
  7. **Step 7(c): the market cart's and the weaver's carrier's tiles tick by tick** for 5 game minutes, using the figure reader (step 5). Every tile each figure stands on must be admitted by the decoded rule for its layer; a tile it refuses must be refused by the rule too. This is the headline Wine-run acceptance.
  8. **Step 7(d): the OQ-103 three clauses** in the same session. (i) A sheep farm served without a road inside a weaver's radius (place the weaver, the sheep farm without a road, confirm wool arrives); (ii) the forester needs a road (place a forester without a road, confirm nothing arrives); (iii) a road connects only at a building's specific entrance tile (place a road at a non-entrance edge, confirm the building is unserved). Each outcome into REPORTS.
  9. **Docs.** `transport.md` §3.3 (updated with step 2's result), §3.4 (updated with step 4's memory compare), new §3.6 "The Wine run (T30b)" with the step 7 outcomes; `annoscene.md` for the search engine's class and its vtable if that is where the method lives; `open-questions.md` OQ-160 updated with the fully-D rule (or exactly what still is open), OQ-171 answered or narrowed, OQ-103 answered or narrowed, OQ-175 either closed or named the next address; `state.md` §5.9's "Proposed rule for T33" rewritten to a FINAL rule when the method is D (or clearly marked as still-draft with the open parts named); REPORTS.md T30b with the acceptance ticked; PR, not merged.
- **Acceptance:** (1) the vtable method is D with its address, or exactly what is still open and the next address; (2) the `+0x434` secondary is D with the writer address, or named open with the next address; (3) the memory compare of step 4 is D (equal / listed differences); (4) the figure reader with its three tests; (5) step 7(c) run (every observed tile admitted by the rule, or every contradiction listed); (6) step 7(d) three clauses answered; (7) nothing of the original in git (no byte dumps, no screenshots); (8) report, PR, not merged. **The owner reads D claims before merge, per the directive.**
- **Not in T30b:** any `src/` change (T33 does it from the final rule); any `rules` option; the weapons carrier (T28); other search modes than the walk (shots: not open yet).

A list of claims, each with its source: when a monument is earned (threshold, duration, per player or per city, endless games), what it costs, what it does (decoration only, or an effect on houses, satisfaction, prestige), whether it can be lost, demolished or rebuilt.

The monument as a building: its class and tick (vtable; T25 (j) says the base class in the production list), its effect if any (a radius on houses' mood?), demolition. The scenario path (`0x10024010`): whether a scenario's monument counts as earned.
  5. **The owner's description, clause by clause:** "a decoration a player earns", "for reaching a threshold in a running game, endless games too", "for example the population satisfied for 30 minutes in a row": each confirmed, corrected or open, with the address or the source.
  6. **Docs.** `docs/re/monuments.md` (new; every line D / likely / open with addresses), OQ-144 and OQ-166 status, a section "What the sim needs" (the state the award needs: counters, timers, per-player flags; the stage it belongs to: the population stages if it reads satisfaction; the scenario-path creation for T31b), REPORTS.md with the acceptance ticked, PR. No `TASKS.md` edit.

### T33 — The decoded walk rule in the sim (replaces the OQ-160 stand-in) · split into **T33.1–T33.4** (round ay), one PR each, in order; T33.1 `DONE` (PR #67 merged on 2026-10-25, CI 20/20); T33.2 `DONE` (merged locally, round ba, GitHub unavailable); T33.3 `DONE` (merged locally, round bb); **T33.4 `READY`** · the main executor · the rule is `docs/sim/state.md` §5.9 ("What T33 implements", clauses 0–8); the evidence is `docs/re/transport.md` §3.4.7–3.4.16 · new OQs from **OQ-200**
- **Rules for all four (read first):** `CLAUDE.md` (determinism: integers only in `sim`, no unordered containers, no global mutable state; no hardcoded limits: the table sizes and the layer count are data with the original's values as defaults, the 4×4 chunk edge is a config value; explicit serialization in `formats`: `bytes.hpp`, `Result<T, E>`, no `reinterpret_cast` of file data). Every mapping in code cites its AnnoWelt address in a comment (§5.9 gives them). The original's tables are **never** copied into the source or fixtures: tests that need them read `game/app` through `anno::test::game_dir` and skip cleanly without it; synthetic tests build their own small tables. Gates of §1 item 4 for every PR (MSVC, ctest with and without the game dir, clang-format 23.1.3, the GCC 13 `-O3 -Werror` check in WSL `Ubuntu` for `sim`/`formats`), mutation checks listed in the report (§1 item 5).
- **T33.1 — The walk tables from `AnnoWelt.dll` (`READY`, round ay; `src/formats` + `anno-dump` + Python oracle + docs).** Branch `task/T33-1-walk-tables`, worktree `anno1503-T33-1`. Steps:
    1. **Spec first:** `docs/formats/annowelt-walk-tables.md`: the minimal PE reading needed (DOS header `e_lfanew` u32 at `0x3c`; `PE\0\0`; COFF `NumberOfSections` u16 at `+6`, `SizeOfOptionalHeader` u16 at `+0x14`; optional header `ImageBase` u32 at `+0x1c` of it; section headers 40 bytes each: `VirtualAddress` u32 `+0xc`, `SizeOfRawData` u32 `+0x10`, `PointerToRawData` u32 `+0x14`), RVA → file offset through the section that contains it, and the three tables with RVA, size and meaning, each tied to its reader address: the mapper `0xc1af8`, 512 × u16 (`0x1009005f`); the layer blocks `0xd1140 + 0x1c00·L`, L = 0..4, `0x1c00` bytes each: bytes `0..0x3ff` admission, `0x400 + 8·code + j` costs (`0x1000dd20`, `0x10006ab0`, `0x10006ab9`); the goal-order table `0xc1160`, 5 rows × 9 entries × 4 bytes (u16 mask, u8 col, u8 row; `0x100036fb..0x1000371c`). Identify the DLL by its COFF `TimeDateStamp` and `SizeOfImage` (record the GOG 2.0.0.5 values from the file) and refuse a different build with a clear error — a modded DLL can be accepted later behind an explicit option (comment the path, PLAN §0.1).
    2. **`src/formats`:** `anno/formats/annowelt.hpp` / `src/annowelt.cpp`: `Result<WalkTables, WalkTablesError> read_walk_tables(std::span<const std::uint8_t> dll)` with `struct WalkTables { std::vector<std::uint16_t> mapper; std::vector<std::vector<std::uint8_t>> layers; std::vector<std::array<GoalEntry, 9>> goal_order; }` (sizes from the spec as defaults, held in vectors, not fixed arrays); field by field through `bytes.hpp`; errors: not a PE, wrong build, RVA outside every section, short file. Unit tests with a synthetic tiny PE built in the test (one section, made-up tables): round trip of every field, each error case; a corpus test that reads `game/app/AnnoWelt.dll` and checks the invariants §5.9 states (P): 512 mapper entries with 33 distinct values, every value's top nibble 0; five layers; `L[7] = 0` and `L[0x80..0x86] = 0` in every layer; `L0[2] = 0xff` and `L3[2] = 0`; `C_0(0) = 10` in layers 0 and 3; `L3[4] = 0xff` with `C_0 = 40`.
    3. **`anno-dump walk-tables <AnnoWelt.dll>`:** prints the build identity, per layer the count of admitted codes below `0x400` and the admission / eight costs of codes 0, 1, 2, 4 (counts and a few numbers only, like `tools/orig/layer_blocks.py --disk-summary`); **Python oracle** `tools/annowelt_tables.py` with the same output from `pefile`-free code (`struct` only), and `tools/agree.py` gets the command.
    4. **Docs and report:** `docs/formats/README.md` one line; REPORTS T33.1 with the gates, the corpus test's output line, `agree.py`'s line, the mutation checks (flip one admission test in the corpus test, flip the RVA of the mapper: both must fail). PR, not merged.
- **T33.2 — Cells per island and the layer lookup (`READY after T33.1`; `src/sim`, pure functions, no stream change).** Branch `task/T33-2-walk-cells`. Steps:
    1. **Where the tables live in the sim:** `WalkTables` (from T33.1) is passed in with the type registry at world creation (the same path `TypeRegistry` takes from `Anlagen.dat`; read `docs/sim/state.md` §4.4 and `src/sim/src/scenario.cpp` / the app's loader for that path) and kept immutable and shared. **Not hashed in T33.2** (round az, answer to the executor's question): the registry's digest is written into the state stream's header (`state_fields.hpp` header block), so adding the walk-table digest there changes the stream layout and every golden; in T33.2 the tables are held beside the registry and nothing is added to the header, `state_hash()` or the save — every existing hash and golden must stay byte-identical (a golden that moves is a bug of this sub-task, not something to re-record). The digest joins the header in **T33.4**, together with `kStateHashVersion` 112 and the re-recorded goldens. `SimConfig::validate` refuses a missing table when a scenario has figures with a walking layer. Synthetic worlds (`synthetic.cpp`) get small synthetic tables.
    2. **The tile record's walk bits (§5.9 clause 1):** a pure function `walk_record(const Island&, const TypeRegistry&, TileXY) -> {code, mask9}`: an object tile → its type's code (the encoder `0x100091f0` from HEAD bytes `0x2f`/`0x30`, the default 4 when both are 0, `AnnoGame 0x1000940b..0x1000942b`; the rotation getter `0x10007dc0` for the high-bit codes) and the rotated 3×3 WALKMASK sample of that footprint tile (`AnnoWelt 0x1007b5e0`, the four rotation formulas of `transport.md` §3.4.13); a tile without an object → code 2, mask 0, or mask `0x1ff` when the water test holds. **The water test:** read `0x1008ff00` (the threshold's initialiser) and `0x10092b40..0x10092c4d` (the mean of the four corner heights from `[I + 0x14]`, compared with `trunc([0x100e34dc] / [0x100ca6fc])`); write what the threshold is (a constant, a function of the island, or a value loaded from a file) into `transport.md` §3.4.13 with the addresses; then check that the sim's `Terrain::height` column is the same corner-height data (`docs/formats/insel.md`, `state.md` §4.8, OQ-060); if both hold, implement the test; if either fails, **stop this sub-step** and write the question (OQ-200) — then empty tiles are code 2 / mask 0 and the deviation is listed. The coast pass `0x10081ce0` is not implemented (round ay: documented deviation, OQ-170).
    3. **Cells:** `struct WalkCell { std::uint16_t low; std::uint16_t high; }` and a per-search cell store over the search rectangle clipped to the island (§5.9 clause 0–1: origin `max(rect.left, island.x)` / `max(rect.top, island.y)`, `0x10002b80`), filled lazily per chunk of `chunk_edge` (config, default 4) tiles on first access: `low = mapper[mask9]`, `high = code`, outside the island 0; then the disc closure (§5.9 clause 5; `0x100037e5..0x10003a84`; the disc fields from the search's area set-up, `0x10002a60`) marks the cells outside the rounded area `(hi & 0xfc07) | 7`. Ordered containers only; the store is local to one search (not state).
    4. **The layer lookup:** `admission(layer, code)` and `cost(layer, code, j)` over `WalkTables`.
    5. **Tests:** synthetic: a 3×3 island with a road, a house (code 4, a WALKMASK with a closed subcell row) at all four rotations, water; the cells' low and high words against hand-computed values. Game-dir tests (skip without it): for `S01_07_01`, the cell of a dirt road `2527` tile is `low 0xfff, code 0`, of a house tile `code 4` with the house's mapped mask; the cart's layer (3) admits code 0 and 4 and not 2; the carrier's (0) admits 0, 2 and 4. REPORTS T33.2; PR, not merged.
- **T33.3 — The search as a pure function (`READY after T33.2`; `src/sim`, no stream change).** Branch `task/T33-3-walk-search`. Implement §5.9 clauses 3–8 as `search(const WalkCells&, const WalkTables&, layer, starts, goals, callback) -> SearchResult` over the cell store of T33.2, with nothing of the stand-in reused. Steps: (1) the open list as an ordered structure keyed by `(u16 score, insertion number)` (clause 7: lowest score first, ties first-in first-out; the original's tree is not copied, its order is); (2) start cells (clause 3): the 3×3 subcell flood from the start position over the start tile's mask bits (island mode 1: `8 + bit` per subcell, `0x1009206c..0x100920e2`), its four edge keys into bits 12–15, code 7, score 0; (3) the default expansion and the four came-from expansions exactly as the table of clause 4 (keys, orders, straight cost `C_dir`, turn cost `C_diag − C_orth`, u16 wrap), the marks `0x80 | dir` with bits 10–15 kept, first discovery decides (no relaxation); (4) goals (clause 5): bit 10 on the goal points' cells, the goal subcell mask, the callback per goal subcell in the order of `goal_order[row]` (row 0..3 by the arrival code, 4 otherwise), the AND of the answers, stop; (5) the result (clause 8): the route as the chain of came-from codes back to a code-7 cell, its tiles, its cost. The finer-route retry (clause 6) is **not** implemented: a `// deviation` comment and the list entry. **Tests:** hand-built grids for each clause: a straight corridor (cost = sum of `C_dir`), an L-shaped corridor (one turn: `+ C_diag − C_orth`), a junction where two routes reach a cell and the first-pushed wins although the other is cheaper (clause 4's "first discovery"), equal scores popping FIFO, a closed code-7 cell, a disc that cuts a goal off, a goal subcell order test per arrival; property tests: the search is deterministic (two runs identical) and never enters a cell with admission 0. REPORTS T33.3; PR, not merged.
- **T33.4 — Into `choose_source`, the goldens, the measurement (`READY`, round bb; `src/sim`, stream change; local-only workflow of round ba).** Branch `task/T33-4-walk-integration` from local `main`. **Steps, each written down before the next:** (1) read `transport.cpp` `choose_source`, `walk_rule` and the route search and write in the report, per caller, what the stand-in is asked (which starts, which goals, what it returns, what `choose_source` does with it) and the matching `walk_search` call (the starts: the home's entrance points as subcell points; the goals: every candidate's entrance subcells; the callback: `Expand` while the job's selection goes on, the selection by `choose_source`'s existing score); (2) implement it for jobs `0x34` (layer 3) and `0x33` (layer 0) only, the other jobs unchanged; (3) a search that reports `complete = false` (an unsupported tile-only object, T33.2) is counted in `TransportStats` and treated as not reached, with a comment naming the deviation; (4) the stream: the walk-table digest in the header next to the registry digest and in the replay log's config check, `kStateHashVersion` 112, `tools/sim_stream.py` updated; (5) the goldens and the corpus re-recorded, the diff's size stated in the report (which goldens moved, how many corpus lines); (6) the measurement. Replace the stand-in in `transport.cpp` (`walk_rule`, the area search in `choose_source` and the route search) by T33.2/T33.3 for the cart (job `0x34`, layer 3) and the workshop carrier (job `0x33`, layer 0): the starts (the home's entrance points), the goals (the candidates' entrance subcells, §5.9 clause 7), the callbacks (the original's selection by score, already in `choose_source`: keep its scoring, change only reachability and route cost). `kStateHashVersion` 112; the walk-table digest added to the stream header next to the registry digest (and to the replay log's config check, `replay.hpp` `registry_digest`'s neighbour); the goldens g2–g6 and `fixtures/synthetic/sim/scenario_corpus.txt` re-recorded in the same commit with the reason; `docs/sim/state.md` §5.9 "As implemented in T33" (the deviations: no finer-route retry, the shore stand-in, the coast pass); OQ-160 / OQ-161 / OQ-171 status lines. **Measurement:** re-run T29's `S01_07_01` and `single36` classification (150 000 ticks): reached markets and producers before/after, in the report as a table; if a number gets worse, name the cause before reporting. REPORTS T33.4; PR, not merged; the orchestrator reviews against the original in the Wine runner (T30b.5–7) before the merge.

### T34 — The float walk: the original's x87 walk reproduced bit-exactly (OQ-162; owner decision 2026-10-24: "float number walk") · stage 1 `READY after T31` (round v; **one subagent**; RE and ADR, docs only) · stage 2 `LATER` (after T33 and the approval of ADR 0002) · stage 1 `PARALLEL-OK` (docs only: `docs/re/transport.md` §4, a new `docs/adr/0002-float-walk.md`, `open-questions.md` OQ-162 and new ones) · branch `task/T34-float-walk-re` from `main` · worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T34` · new OQs from **OQ-195** (to 199)
- **Why:** the owner chose the float walk, so trips take the original's time. The ground rule stays (CLAUDE.md, `lint_sim.py`): `src/sim` computes in integers and no float enters hashed state. So the walk's floats become their bit patterns in the state, and the arithmetic becomes an integer emulation of exactly what the x87 did: the original's result on every platform. That needs the precision mode and every rounding point, or the "float" would not be the original's float.
- **Stage 1 steps (in order; §1 item 7):**
  1. **Set up** from `main`.
  2. **The precision and rounding mode the walk runs under (D):** every `fldcw` in the game modules and the control words it loads; the DirectDraw / Direct3D set-up (`SetCooperativeLevel` flags `DDSCL_FPUSETUP` 0x1000 / `DDSCL_FPUPRESERVE` 0x2000, any device-creation FPU flags); the MSVC 6 runtime start-up. State the mantissa precision (24 / 53 / 64 bits) and the rounding mode while the walk (`0x10057ae0` and its callees) runs, and how each float → int conversion rounds (MSVC 6's `_ftol` switches the control word to truncation: check every conversion in the walk).
  3. **The exact operation sequence of the walk (D):** from `transport.md` §4 (time = `u16(t − stamp) · 0.2f`; `time · speed` along f32 segments of `count / 3` tiles; diagonals × `1.41421354f`; the position three floats; the rest of the time carried to the next action): every operation in order, every load and store width (a `fstp dword` rounds to binary32, a value kept on the x87 stack does not), every constant's bit pattern, every compare and conversion. Pseudo-code with addresses in §4.
  4. **The path the walk follows** (the sub-cell segments, OQ-161, from T30 step 2): what a segment is. If T30 has not read it, write the dependency and go on.
  5. **ADR `docs/adr/0002-float-walk.md`:** the decision (the owner's) and the model: a small integer soft-float in `src/sim` (binary32 add, sub, mul, div, compare and int conversions, with the precision and exponent-range behaviour of step 2; round to nearest even unless step 2 says otherwise), the state fields as `u32` bit patterns, the lint unchanged; the rejected alternative (native `float` with strict flags: x87 and SSE differ in precision and exponent range, and the lint forbids it); the stream bump and goldens; the test plan (the soft-float against the host's IEEE `float` in the tests, which may use floats, over ≥ 10^8 random operands per operation plus the edge cases: denormals, overflow, ties; the walk against a hand-derived x87 trace; later against the original's figure positions from T30 step 7's reader); the performance budget (the walk's share of a tick before and after, `bench_sim`).
  6. **Docs.** §4, OQ-162 status ("decided 2026-10-24 (owner): the float walk; the model per ADR 0002"), REPORTS.md with the acceptance ticked, PR. No `TASKS.md` edit.
- **Stage 1 acceptance:** (1) the precision and rounding mode D with addresses, or open with what was searched; (2) the walk's operation sequence D, every rounding point named; (3) ADR 0002 with the test plan; (4) nothing of the original in git; (5) report, PR, not merged.
- **Stage 2 (one line):** the soft-float module and its tests, the walk on it, the position and time fields as bit patterns, a stream bump with every golden re-recorded, a cart's trip compared with the original's in the Wine runner.

### T35 — `Result::value()` and `error()` must not throw in Release (a policy hole found by the 2026-10-24 snapshot) · `READY after T30b.8` (round ac; **one sub-task, `src/formats` only**) · `PARALLEL-OK` (touches `src/formats/include/anno/formats/result.hpp`, one test file, `docs/formats/README.md` one sentence) · branch `task/T35-result-abort` from `main` · worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T35` · no new OQ
- **Why:** `Result<T, E>::value()` and `error()` guard the wrong arm with `assert(ok())`. Under `NDEBUG` (the Release configuration the `msvc` preset builds and tests) the assert is gone and `std::get` throws `std::bad_variant_access` out of `formats`, which CLAUDE.md forbids ("no exceptions in `formats`/`sim`"). No caller is known to misuse it; the rule must hold regardless.
- **Steps (in order; §1 items 7 and 11):**
  1. Worktree: `git worktree add /c/Users/altin/anno1503-T35 -b task/T35-result-abort origin/main`; build: `cmake --preset msvc` then `cmake --build --preset msvc` (foreground; the first build takes several minutes).
  2. In `result.hpp` replace each `assert(ok());` / `assert(!ok());` by an unconditional check that calls `std::abort()` on the wrong arm (a small private helper `require(bool)` with `[[unlikely]]`; include `<cstdlib>`; keep `<cassert>` only if still used). Keep the three `value()` overloads and `error()` as they are otherwise; no other file changes.
  3. Add a death test only if doctest supports it on all three compilers (it does not: skip) — instead add a case in `src/formats/tests/` that checks `ok()` and `operator bool` on both arms and that `value()` on an ok result and `error()` on an error result return the stored objects (the positive paths); state in the report that the abort path is not unit-tested and why.
  4. Build and run: `ctest --preset msvc -j 16 -R formats` with and without `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR`; clang-format 23.1.3 on the two files; the GCC 13 `-O3 -Werror` check in WSL (`tools/check_unix_warnings.sh`).
  5. `docs/formats/README.md`: one sentence under the `Result` contract: a misuse aborts, it never throws. REPORTS.md section T35 with the gates' output lines; PR, not merged.
- **Acceptance:** (1) no `assert` guards the variant access; a misuse aborts; (2) the positive-path test; (3) the gates of step 4 pasted; (4) the README sentence; (5) report, PR, not merged.

### T22 — The figure removal path as the original's: the four OQ-131 deviations · **`DONE`** (merged as 60c259a on 2026-10-23 q, PR #33, CI green; §4) · was `READY` (round n) · `PARALLEL-OK` with T20 stage 1 (T22: `src/sim` only (`figure.*`, `scheduler.cpp`, `scheduler_internal.hpp`, `config.*`, `state_fields.hpp`, `world.hpp`, `invariants.cpp`, the sim tests), `fixtures/synthetic/sim/`, `tools/sim_stream.py`, `docs/sim/state.md`, `docs/open-questions.md`, `docs/PLAN.md`, `REPORTS.md`) · branch `task/T22-figure-removal-fix` from `main` (at or after 7c13f3b) · worktree `C:\Users\altin\anno1503-T22`
- **Why:** T21 read the original's removal path (`docs/re/annowelt.md` "Unit registry: creation, removal and the figure destructor"; OQ-131 resolved; `state.md` §4.9 "Deviations, to fix in S13 or S15"). The S12 stand-in differs in four points that are not feel questions (the owner decided 2026-10-23: fix them in one task after S13, not inside S13 and not at S15). Gameplay reason: today a production building gets its carrier counter back 7 500 ticks (15 s of game time) after the figure's end instead of at the end, so the next carrier leaves late; and a seam that releases at the end is released twice.
- **Do** (every rule with its address from OQ-131 / `annowelt.md`; no new RE is needed):
  1. **Release at the end, not at the destroy** (D `0x1003b6e0`, the end behaviour's call of home slot `+0x2c`): one function `end_figure(registry, figure, t, effects)` in `figure.hpp` that (a) emits the `ReleaseCounter` for `home` / `home_counter` (nothing when `home` is already none), (b) clears `Figure::home` (so a second call changes nothing, as the original's nulled `+0x78`), (c) sets the stamp to `u16(t)` and appends the figure to `expiry`. The D12 interim rule calls it in phase B; a behaviour seam reaches it through `SerialWorld` (the seam writes only its own figure and its Effects, C2). `Scheduler::destroy_figure` **stops releasing** (D `0x10097520` nulls the entry and deletes the unit, nothing else). `check_invariants` §9.6 counts, per building counter, the figures that still **have** that home (a figure on `expiry` has none).
  2. **The interim return goes through `expiry`** (D: `0x10034844` Remove(unit, 0) appends to list `+0x48`; phase D and `0x10097720` serve the pooled sector records, never figures): the D12 rule at `u32(t − born) >= interim_figure_return_ticks` calls `end_figure` instead of `mark_figure_for_delete`; the slot stays occupied until phase C frees it at the first advance with `u16(t' − stamp) >= figure_expiry_ticks`. **Drop** `Figure::marked_for_delete`, `FigureRegistry::deferred`, `mark_figure_for_delete` and phase D (no figure path reaches them; the pooled sector objects are O-17 / OQ-134 and get their own model if they are ever ported). Dead hashed state is worse than a smaller stream: say so in §5.9 and §9.3 with the addresses, and keep the phase letters A–D of the original in the comments (D = "pooled records, not modelled").
  3. **`SimConfig::figure_min_growth = 100`** (D `0x10099185..0x100991a1`), declared next to `list_min_growth`, validated like it (1 .. `kListSizeBound`, the same reason F22), used by `add_figure`'s `SlotParams` instead of `list_min_growth`; the registry's `list_initial_size` stays 1 (A7). Derive by hand from `SlotList::insert`'s growth rule the vector length after the first growth with 100 and with 10, and assert the first (and that the phase-B visit count follows `n`).
  4. **Rename `Figure::created_stamp` → `expiry_stamp`** everywhere (the tick of the figure's end, the unit's frozen `+8`), bytes unchanged; `tools/sim_stream.py` follows the name and the dropped fields.
  5. **Stream version 108 → 109** (`kStateHashVersion`; reasons: the header scope gains `figure_min_growth`, the figure record loses `marked_for_delete`, the registry loses `deferred`), G1–G5 re-baselined and `g2.log`..`g5.log` re-recorded with the reason in `goldens.txt` (every golden with figures moves because `n` changes; state which goldens moved for which of the four points: run the four commits one at a time and record the moved set per commit in REPORTS.md). `tools/sim_stream.py --check` agrees.
  6. Docs: `state.md` §4.9 (the deviations paragraph becomes "fixed in T22" with the date), §5.9 (phase D, the end path), §9.3 / §9.6, §15 "T22 as implemented" (a short section after S13's); OQ-131 status "fixed 2026-10-23 (T22)" and the four change items marked done; `figure.hpp` comments; PLAN status line; a REPORTS.md section; a PR.
- **Acceptance (written before work starts):**
  1. Tests (a new `figure_removal_test.cpp`, or the figure cases of `world_events_test.cpp` extended; names as given): **"release at the end, not at the destroy"** (two figures of one building out, counter 2; the first ends at tick T through a seam that calls `end_figure`: counter 1 at T, the figure on `expiry` with `expiry_stamp = u16(T)`, `home` none; at T + 7 500 the slot is free and the counter is **still 1**, never 0); **"a second release does nothing"** (`end_figure` twice, and a seam's own `ReleaseCounter` after `end_figure`: the counter moves once; `check_invariants` clean after each); **"the interim return goes on expiry"** (D12 on with N: at the phase-B visit at or after born + N the counter is released and the figure is on `expiry`; a figure spawned 1 000 ticks later takes the **next** slot; the old slot is reused only after T + 7 500; `deferred` no longer exists); **"figure registry grows by 100"** (the length after the first growth, by the rule derived in Do 3; with `figure_min_growth = 10` the old length, so the test tells the two apart); **"a figure whose home was demolished ends without a release"** (A6 / T21: `end_figure` on a figure with `home` none emits nothing and still goes on `expiry`); the §9.6 invariant case: a figure on `expiry` is not counted, and a counter above the figures with that home is reported.
  2. The S7, S11 and S12 figure tests pass with their numbers re-derived where the slot numbering or the counter timing changed (name each changed assertion and why in REPORTS.md; "it moved" is not a reason).
  3. **Mutation evidence** (the whole sim suite per mutant, MSVC): `destroy_figure` releasing again fails "release at the end" (the counter 2 → 1 → 0 after the destroy); `end_figure` not clearing `home` fails "a second release does nothing"; the D12 rule freeing the slot in the same advance fails "the interim return goes on expiry" (the next slot); `figure_min_growth` read as `list_min_growth` fails "figure registry grows by 100" and G1–G5; `expiry_stamp` set to the spawn tick (the old meaning) fails the "release at the end" timing.
  4. `validate_figures`: on `expiry` iff `expiry_stamp` present; a figure with a stamp **and** a home is refused (a state the end path cannot leave); no figure refers to a building counter it does not hold (the §9.6 rule read back on load).
  5. Gates of §1 item 4 through the heavy-job lock: MSVC build; ctest with and without the game dir (full run in the foreground; if the memory guard stops it, `-R anno_sim` and the fact stated); clang-format 23.1.3; WSL GCC 13.3 and Clang 18 `-O3 -Werror`; `lint_sim.py`; `sim_stream.py --check`; `anno_sim_replay_g2..g5_run` pass on the re-recorded logs.
  6. Docs and the PR as in Do 6; commits one concern each (the rename alone, the growth alone, the end path with phase D's removal, the stream bump with the goldens, the docs), so the moved goldens are attributable.
- **Not in T22:** the pooled sector objects (O-17, OQ-134), the unit's u16 slot cap (our wider `Slot` is the widening), any behaviour seam body.

For a 1-of-N tag or flag the family is `and r, imm`, `test r, imm`, `test byte[r+k], imm`, `test word[r+k], imm`, `cmp byte[r+k], imm`, `cmp word[r+k], imm`, `movzx r, byte/word[r+k]` → `test`/`cmp`/`and r, imm`, and conditional branches on the flags of any masked load from a chunk pointer. This family is already written out in the leading theories of §3.4.3; turning each into a grep is the T30b.3f step.

(3) Protocol lesson for the executor (same as round ag's lesson, now a protocol invariant): **the order of 4-dword buffer stores is determined by where `esp` is at each store, not by the order of `add` steps between stores.** Every store between a `push` and the next instruction uses a frame shifted by 4; label stores by frame-aware buffer offset, not by the register name's position in an `add` chain. This is now the second task where the same slip was caught and corrected in place; a third would warrant a required re-check step in the sub-task template.

None is the walkability yes/no, which keeps the real decision one step away. The most interesting single fact after round ah is that W, X, Y share a base `0x100c135c` referenced from four constructor regions, suggesting a small inheritance tree with a common "chunk-window consumer" interface; T30b.3d or a later sub-task could name the base's slots to understand the shared contract. The tuple matches §3.3's `(x0, y0, x1, y1)` for `0x1000daf0`, which is the SAME shape the executor correctly cross-referenced. Both corrections live in `transport.md` §3.3.2 with an "Orchestrator correction round ag" marker; the headline (chunk-cache mark, world pointer, not a yes/no) is unchanged. **Decisions:** (1) merged in place rather than requiring a rewrite — the two errors are small and the headline stands; the executor's thoroughness in verifying `0x100a52d0`, the layer-block first dwords, and the §3.3.1 label fixes outweighs the two docs slips. (2) T30b.3c opened for `0x100011c0` (the other pointer-reader the sweep now surfaces) and T30b.3d opened for the cross-module `--reloc` sweep (`AnnoGame.dll` / `AnnoScene.dll` / `AnnoDisplay.dll` for `0x100d1140..0x100d9d40`); both small, both docs only, both PARALLEL-OK with T30b.4. (4) Round-ad protocol lesson added for the executor: `ret N` is the function's own epilogue byte sequence (`c2 NN NN`), not the push count of an inner `call`; and the order of 4-dword buffer stores follows where `esp` is at each store (every `push` shifts the frame), not the order of `add eax, 4` / `add ecx, 4` arithmetic — both corrections are in the round-ag header and are the next sub-task's handrail.

Written into `transport.md` §3.3, OQ-175/176/177/178, `state.md` §5.9 (the "two tables" sentence struck through). Consequence: T30's reading of `0x1000daf0` as the layer-table reader was wrong (a this-adjustment of 4 bytes turned two coordinates into two tables); the review of round aa did not catch it either, because I re-read the instructions, not the class layout. T30b.3 rewritten to read `0x1000dd60`.
  - Game-developer assessment: the real rule is now one method away again, but this time the method is the right kind of thing: a test of a figure's float position against a window of tile records and a per-layer table. That is exactly the shape a walk rule has, and its float input confirms the owner's T34 decision (the float walk) is on the path.

- **Rounds v-z reviewed; T30 merged as ad9a314 (2026-10-24 aa, PR #43, head ce97d51, docs only, CI 20/20).** The regular orchestrator's independent read of what the executor session merged and wrote as orchestrator. **Code (on `main`):** T27b's `check_against_config` recomputes `walk_class` under `cfg.layout` (`types.cpp`, the road flag → 1, a list for the prodtype → 2, else 0) and refuses a mismatch; `finish` sets the field before the check; the registry test covers accept, accept-equivalent and refuse. T29's `TransportStats*` sits in `TickContext` and in a non-state `World` member (named in the field-list exclusion comment), null by default, routed only by `set_transport_stats`: not hashed, not saved, as claimed. T26's fix-up sizes the trade lists to the entries read and folds a non-default slot count into `Head2FieldNotMapped`: the representation the round-v review asked for. CI: every merge commit and every board commit on `main` green; 8d0a0ec's run was in progress at the time of this read (docs only). **RE (re-done, not re-read):** `0x1000daf0` disassembled: `mov cx, [esi]` / `mov dx, [esi − 2]` after `add esi, 4` are the two u16s at cell offsets 0 and 2; the tables `[eax + 0x430]` / `[eax + 0x434]`; the buffer `[primary + p0, secondary + p2, primary + p0 + 8, secondary + p2 + 8]` at `esp + 0x10..0x20`; `ecx = [this + 0x858]`, `call [edx + 0xec]`, `ret 4` — all as §3.3 says, **except the arguments: five are pushed (`8`, `cell + 4`, `&buffer`, `0`, `[this + 0x85c]`), the docs listed four and wrote `this + 0x85c` for the loaded value.** Corrected on `main` (`transport.md` §3.3 and §3.6, OQ-175, `state.md` §5.9). The mapper `0x100c1af8` re-counted with `pefile`: 33 distinct u16 outputs, `0xfff` 123 times, 27 of the 33 not multiples of 8, high nibbles {0, 3, 4, 7, 8, b, c, f}: as §3.6 (b) says; its "1 byte of space after the read" sentence was wrong arithmetic (`0xfff + 8 = 0x1007` of `0x1c00`) and is corrected: the meaning of `0xfff` is open. The five blocks: 42–87 distinct byte values each, layer 3 the sparsest (75 bytes `0xff`, 3 277 zero): the "not a plain yes/no" sentence of the report holds. The Anlagen counts re-run with the executor's script: 921 entries, 35 with `0x4000`, every named-road WALKMASK 12 zero bytes: as §3.2. **Correction (round ab):** I let the executor's "36 named roads, 26 / 10" and "33 named + 2 piers" pass without adding the rows up; the T30b.1 executor did (Q1): the named-road rows are 35 by name plus the 2 piers = **37**, split **26 / 11** (`2505–2508`, `2517`, `2525–2528`, `2536–2537` carry `0x1000`), and the 35 flagged types are 24 named roads + 2 piers + 9 unnamed `BRUECKE` pieces (`3955`, `3956`, `3961`, `3990–3993`, `3997`, `3998`). Lesson for me: a count I "re-run" is only re-run when I add the rows up myself. **Board and protocol:** the four decisions the executor-as-orchestrator listed stand (T27b's `walk_class`-only scope accepted with a note; the scope rule confirmed as the amended item 7); its honesty about its own over-claims (three in T27) and the "independent read before a merge of D tags" rule are kept. Found by the read: `agent.py` (T30 report, T30b steps) does not exist in `tools/orig/`; the reader is `backend.py` `read` through `live.py`; `transport.md` §3.6 sits before §3.5 (left; the Wine run becomes §3.7); `PLAN.md` had not caught up with T27b, T29 and T30 (done in this round); §1 carries two items numbered 7 (noted in the new item 11, not renumbered: the task sections cite "item 7" for the steps rule).

The owner's "100 % sure" is the right bar here, because this rule decides how every player lays out a town (OQ-103): a wrong stand-in would be felt in the first ten minutes of play. T30b is therefore split into small, checkable steps for the new executor, the Wine run included; T33 waits for §5.9 to be final.

The windowed wrapper is the answer either way; place its window on `\\.\DISPLAY2` (the primary, x 0..2559). **What the attempt found:** the launcher opens at 2560x1440 on the second monitor (titled `Anno1503`, 17 threads), a desktop capture of the main monitor is black; `/w` gave a "Display Error" dialog (188x130); `AnnoFrame.dll` is not loaded at the menu, so the memory read waits for a running scenario; no process left, hashes unchanged. **Owner decision 2026-10-21 r: a DirectDraw wrapper, windowed, on the main monitor; cnc-ddraw first (open source, MIT; made for DirectDraw games; windowed mode with a window position in its `ddraw.ini`), dgVoodoo2 if cnc-ddraw does not run the game.** Rules: the wrapper's `ddraw.dll` and its ini go **only into `C:\anno1503-original\`** (never `game/app`, never git); record the wrapper's version, download URL, SHA-256 and licence in `original-harness.md`; the copy's hash check then lists the added files separately (the game's 20 files must stay equal to `game/app`).
