The owner is at the PC for the session (cursor for the menu while the scripted input is not there yet); say beforehand what will run and for how long, as rule (c) asks. **The smoke run then:** start, a screenshot of the window taken by the script (check it is not black: look at it, point 10), through the menu to a running scenario (the owner clicks, or `SendInput` to the window if the owner agrees in that session), the module bases (`EnumProcessModules`: do the DLLs load at their preferred bases?), the world tick read twice and growing, close, hashes. Then the report; `tools/orig/smoke.py` committed (the owner's message said it is on the branch; it is not in PR #13 yet).
- **T3 stage 3, owner look (2026-10-21 q, `Single_24`, RTX 4090, 1280x800 then maximized to 2560x1369, zoom groups 0-4):** "looks good so far. just some minor things left we can leave for now though." **Merged as a1dd296 (PR #9).** The owner's frame log is clean at 1280x800 (window thread avg 0.3-0.5 ms, worst under 3.2 ms after the first frame; a zoom-out to level 3 received 917 chunks and level 4 2318 in an interval with no frame above 2.8 ms). **Minor points from the log for the T3 follow-up (measure and explain first; fix where the cause is ours):** (a) **`sync scan 6.72` ms** in one frame at group 1 -> 2 after maximizing (2560x1369, 478 chunks cached): the scan of the cache for the frame's keys should not cost that; find what grows with the cache and bound it; (b) **`fluid` on the window thread, 1.6-1.9 ms every few frames at group 0 and 3.91 ms once at 2560x1369**: which part of `FluidGpu::update` runs there (recentre writes, bathymetry upload?) and whether it can move to the job thread or be spread over frames; (c) **the resize frame, `draw prologue 9.18` ms** after maximizing (the surface reconfigure and the targets of the new size; the 146.8 ms of window events before it are the OS's resize): what is in it now that the planes are prewarmed (OQ-093), and whether the targets can be made ahead; (d) the first frame (`sea 2.69`, `fluid 2.69`, 9.2 ms in all): accepted as a first frame; (e) gaps of 10-23 ms between frames with a busy time of 1-2 ms and "waiting for the display" up to 18.6 ms: say whether this is the present path (the window may be on the monitor of the other adapter: the 500 Hz monitor is driven by the Radeon iGPU, the frames are made on the RTX 4090) and what the viewer could do about it (nothing, if it is the compositor's copy); not a fix in this task.
- **T3 stage 3 complete (2026-10-21 p, orchestrator verification of 6cad402..1b0aa1d, PR #9, CI green on all ten jobs). Approved; merged after the owner's look.** Verified: **fix 2:** `ChunkPtr` (immutable shared chunks) in the cache, the uploads and the jobs; `plan_jobs` puts a derived key's missing children before it, depth first, and submits the parent only with all four children cached (shared, not copied), the children kept until then; capped at 512 keys a frame (the plan runs on the window thread; reason stated); `submit` skips chunks finished but not yet taken (no double build); `fill_cache` builds level by level from the same plan; the recursive `render_chunk` stays as the from-nothing reference and the fill-time baseline (`derive_from_cache = false`). A derived chunk has no `flow_waiting` (`reduce_chunks_2x` drops it), so a child whose river flow arrives later cannot make its parent stale every frame: checked. Fill time 3.5x (1 thread) and 10x (16 threads) faster on the canyon island, the build-once test over a zoom-out sweep: accepted. **Commit 1:** bilinear above scale 1, forced Area below 1 is Box, Area renormalised beyond 8 taps; `zoom_hold_floor` holds a zoom-out at the point the coarser level would be taken while it is not ready (only a zoom-out; grid at most 2.2x the window). **Uploads a plane at a time under `upload_budget_ms`**, a rebuilt chunk (other pointer under the key) restarts, uploads nothing wants any more are dropped and their planes pooled. **The wave grid is made on the job thread** (`make_grid`: texture and bind-group creation only, the device is thread-safe; the job is joined in `~Job` before the members it uses are destroyed: checked). Ships at derived levels (the built ancestor's frame box-reduced in the sprite shader, GPU = CPU, both adapters), the cross-fade (OQ-076), prewarm on resize (OQ-093), the margin velocity from `pan_centre`, the scale-1 resample bug, the timestamp sanity limit: accepted. **Orchestrator look (point 10):** `--png` of the canyon island at group 2, scale 0.55 (Box) and 1.3 (Bilinear), 1280x800, RTX 4090: no doubled rows or columns, coast, river and cliffs clean at 0.55, soft and even at 1.3. **Three small follow-ups (first commit on the next branch from `main`):** (1) **`--png` cannot show a derived level** (`--group` takes 0-2 only), so nobody can look at levels 3+ outside the viewer: let `--png --group` take every level of the scene's table (the same chunks the viewer draws, reduced from the cache), with a CLI test, and look at levels 3-5 of the canyon island and `Single_24` before reporting (point 10); (2) **a dropped upload can free a chunk on the window thread:** `Staged::hold` keeps the chunk alive; when the cache trimmed it meanwhile, `drop_staged` (and `staged.erase` after the last plane) releases the last reference on the window thread (~1 ms per chunk, the reason the reaper exists): hand the holds to the reaper (`ChunkWorkers::discard`) or say why it cannot happen; (3) **offscreen targets:** `fits` keeps a target up to 3x the view per axis and grows it by a quarter: at 2560x1440 with the 2.2x grid that is up to ~7000 x 4000 texels per target, held while zoomed in again; state the VRAM held (`under`, `scene`) at the worst point in the README and shrink after a few seconds below a third of the area, or say why not (it matters on the iGPU, whose memory is shared). **For the owner:** the cross-fade, the hold and `--zoom-max-upscale` are on by default; ships beyond 10 halvings are not drawn (under a pixel).
- **T3 stage 3 fix 1 (2026-10-21 n, orchestrator verification of 0e62743, PR #9; CI green except the known flaky macOS tier test in one of two runs).** Verified: `zoom_level_for` takes the coarsest level whose scale is <= `max_upscale` (default 1), the finer one as soon as the scale would exceed it, the coarser one only at 0.95 of its tile width (0.95 instead of 1 / 1.05: accepted); a finer level not ready is bridged by the next coarser ready one, magnified; the viewer's "keep drawing" check uses the same function with every level ready. `ResampleFilter::Area` in `scale_fs` is the coverage-weighted mean over the footprint, equal to the CPU model within 1 on both GPUs (adapters named). The box at 0.5 of a derived level equals the coarser level within 1 on the ground; over objects whose alpha varies inside a 2 x 2 block it cannot be exact (alpha-over and the mean do not commute), stated in `iso.md`: accepted. `GpuFrame::frame_gpu_ms()` covers the resample pass: item (a) done. **Cost (README):** RTX 4090 worst point 2.8 ms p50 / 4.7 ms max at 2560x1440 (was 1.0), under the 8 ms line. **Radeon iGPU: 31 ms at 1280x800 and 195 ms at 2560x1440 at the worst point; but the old rule was already 9.8 / 72 ms there, and even scale 1 costs 3.6 / 13.4 ms** (enhanced water): the iGPU's problem is the compositor's cost, not this fix, and `--zoom-max-upscale` cannot rescue it. Note in the table: group 2 at 0.475 costs twice group 1 at 0.452 for the same pixels (31 against 17 ms), and 12 ns per pixel at 2560x1440 against 6.5 at 1280x800: a cost that grows with the world area shown (the wave simulation window, the coast passes), not only with pixels; that is T14's first question. **Two small things for fix 2's commit:** (1) "a coarser level that is not ready is waited for" lets the composed grid grow without bound while the zoom keeps going out (a fast zoom-out right after start, before the derived sea patterns are built): bound it (hold the zoom at the current level's 0.95 / 2.1 until the coarser level is ready, or the 8192 cap stated and tested) and test it; (2) `scale_fs` Area loops at most 8 x 8 grid pixels, so `ANNO_VIEW_RESAMPLE=area` at a scale below 1/7 drops weight: fall back to Box below scale 1 when Area is forced, or state the limit. **Owner decision 2026-10-21 (OQ-095 item 5), after the look at fix 1 with area, bilinear and the old nearest: "bilinear works very well": bilinear is the magnifying default.** Fix 2's commit switches `resample_filter` above scale 1 from Area to Bilinear (Area stays selectable and tested), with `iso.md` and the usage text. **Owner decision 2026-10-21 on T14:** "Agree with your recommendation": T14 is queued after T3 stage 3; it does not block T3.
- **T3 stage 3, derived levels draw (2026-10-21 m, orchestrator verification of 60b902c..e6bdd3e, PR #9 green on all ten jobs) and the owner's look. Not merged: two fixes first.** **Fix 1, the pixelated picture (owner-visible).** The owner's screenshot (title "zoom group 2") has one column and one row in every 7-8 doubled: group 2's grid magnified 1.13x with the nearest filter. `zoom_advance` shows the level *nearest in ratio*, so the composed grid is magnified by up to about 1.45x half of the time, and `resample_filter` magnifies with `Nearest`: at a non-integer factor that doubles every second to seventh pixel row and column, and it shows a coarser level than the zoom needs. With the derived levels this is half of the whole far range. The T3 design said "choosing the group whose tile size is closest *above* the current one (downscale, not upscale)"; stages 1-2 built nearest-in-ratio and the orchestrator's review did not catch it. Required: (1) `zoom_advance` composes on the coarsest level whose tile width is at least the zoom (scale <= 1, resampled by `Box`), switching to the finer level as soon as the scale would exceed 1 and to the coarser level only once the zoom is 5 % below the coarser level's tile width (hysteresis on the coarse side only); a level not ready yet is bridged by the next one, magnified. At scale 0.5 the box of the finer derived level equals the coarser level (it is its 2x box reduction), so a switch between derived levels does not change the picture: say so in `iso.md` and test it on one view (the two frames within 1 per channel). (2) Where magnifying cannot be avoided (above group 0's tile width, up to its sqrt 2; a finer level not ready yet), an **area filter**: each target pixel the coverage-weighted mean of the grid pixels its footprint overlaps (at most 2 x 2 for scale >= 1): equal-sized crisp pixels, no doubled rows; a new `ResampleFilter::Area` in `scale_fs` and in the CPU model, GPU = CPU on both GPUs. `ANNO_VIEW_RESAMPLE` keeps its A/B role. **Owner, 2026-10-21, with `ANNO_VIEW_RESAMPLE=bilinear` on the PR #9 build: "seems better already"** (this confirms the cause: bilinear magnification hides the doubled rows, softly); so the magnifying filter is the owner's pick at the fix-1 look (OQ-095 item 5): area by default, bilinear one switch away, both GPU = CPU. (3) `--zoom-max-upscale <factor>` (default 1.0; 1.45 gives the build the owner saw), for the A/B and for weak GPUs, with the CLI test. (4) Cost, measured and in the README: the composed grid is now up to about 2.2x the window per axis (4.9x the pixels) instead of 1.45x; GPU ms of the frame (compositor and resample) at the worst point (zoom 5 % below a coarser level's tile width) against the old rule, 1280x800 and 2560x1440, on the RTX 4090 and on the Radeon iGPU; the chunks visible and cached there (the cache cap and the prefetch must cover the finer level's 4.9x area; show that no visible chunk is trimmed). The design condition "composed grid within 1.45x the window" becomes "within 2.2x"; still O(window). (5) Tests: the zoom sweep over all levels keeps the scale within [1 / (2.1 x 1.05), 1] below group 0 once the levels are ready (the existing 0.68..1.46 sweep changes accordingly), the switch rules, the area filter. Then **the owner looks again** (OQ-095 item 5, nearest vs bilinear, is answered by this or asked again with the build). **Fix 2, derived chunks are rebuilt from group 2 every time.** `render_chunk` at level k builds its four children recursively and drops them: a level-k chunk costs 4^(k-2) group-2 builds (64 for level 5, seconds on one worker at a p95 of 69 ms per group-2 chunk), two neighbouring chunks share nothing, and each zoom-out to the next level rebuilds the whole scene's group-2 chunks again, although the level above is usually in the cache because it was just shown. That is the cost condition 3 was about, and the approved design reduced from the level above at upload. Required: a derived chunk is reduced from the four chunks of the level above **as they are in the cache**; only missing children are built, and they go into the cache as ordinary chunks of their level (normal trim rules), not dropped. Either the GPU reduce pass at upload (the design; it exists and is exact) or the CPU reference on a worker with shared ownership of the cached children (no plane copies on the window thread; immutable chunks behind a shared pointer); the window thread never runs a CPU reduction. Test: a zoom-out sweep from group 2 to the minimum builds every group-2 chunk of the scene at most once while it stays cached (count builds per key). Measure the first zoom-out fill time (condition 4) before and after, 1 and 16 threads, canyon island and `Single_24`. **Accepted as built:** the derived sea pattern (the group-2 period 320 x 160 halves five times without drift, measured), bounds halved and rounded outwards, units, water objects and the wave simulation off at derived levels (the sprite scale is the next commit), `resident_level_chunks` with the test on and off, the level table in `FrameRenderer::sync_levels`, GPU = CPU at levels 3-5 on both GPUs with the adapters named (point 9 met). Still open from the owner's logs: the first frame builds group 2's sea on the window thread (`sea 26.12` and `19.92` ms, item (d)); after maximizing the window to 2560x1369, one frame of `draw prologue 8.35` ms (the resize) and one of `sync uploads 10.94` ms (a burst of chunk uploads at the larger window: the per-frame upload budget does not bound it in ms), and one `finish 6.64` ms: the busy frames above 8 ms go into item (e)'s measurements with their cause, and the upload burst is bounded by the budget in ms, not only in count.
- **T1f, CI test of the bounded blur and chamfer (2026-10-21 m, orchestrator verification of f23a7b7, PR #10).** Merged as ed27849. The functions moved to `river_window.hpp` (`detail`, whole-rect switch and margin shrink), synthetic tests at the canvas edges, with the closing, sea at exactly the farthest blending distance and seams at the window border; one pixel less margin changes the result in both. The margins were tightened to the exact reach: blur 2r (+1 with the closing): verified (the inputs of every pass reach at most r + 1 in that pass's axis, less than the margin for r >= 1, so the edge repeat repeats zeros); chamfer max(ceil(reach) - 1, 2 floor(seam)): verified, including a case the text does not state: a pixel the seam turns into river has its `dist` read in the final loop; its value is at most its `to_sea` <= `seam` (the seam seeds are `dist` seeds and the chamfer is the same), so its ball lies within 2 floor(seam) of the river box, inside the window. **Follow-up, one small commit on the T1f branch:** add that sentence to `iso.md`; and the flaky test `chunk profile: off without a sink, nested stages counted exclusively` (failed once on MSVC, `render_test.cpp:358`, passed on the push run of the same commit): the stage times are converted to ms per interval and summed, `elapsed` once, so with no clock tick between `start` and the scope the sum can exceed `elapsed` by rounding; compare with a tolerance of 1e-6 ms and say why.
- **T1f item 2b (2026-10-21 l, orchestrator verification of 02c0469..14ff186; PR #8 green). Merged as 92257ae.** Verified in the code: `blurred_alpha` works on the alpha's bounding box grown by 2r + 2 (the closing reaches 1 pixel and the two box passes per axis r each, so values beyond 2r + 1 stay zero at every stage, and the extra zero ring makes the window's edge repeat equal to the true zeros; clipped at the canvas, the edge repeat is the real one); `blend_rivers_into_sea` works on the river pixels' bounding box grown by ceil(max(reach, 2 seam)) + 2: a windowed chamfer distance can only be larger than the true one, every path cheaper than `reach` stays within `reach` of its river pixel, so every value the code reads below its threshold is exact, and a seam pixel and its sea seed lie within 2 seam of the box; the two seed loops merged without a change of order; the nearest-cover ties are decided by the same scan order over the same pixels. Width-1 island test added (item 2's gap). Result on an idle machine: river stages at 16 threads 2.7-6.4 to 2.0-4.0 ms, chunk mean 3-14 % lower, p95 at 16 threads group 2 104 to 69 ms (canyon) and 84 to 56 ms (`Single_24`); the 16-thread rows of items 1 and 2 are in the README (item 2 had already taken group 0 at 16 threads from 85-93 to 39-51 ms). **Gap, follow-up:** the equality evidence is the game-file digests only (the synthetic golden test covers `sea::water_field`, not the scene's blur and chamfer), so CI cannot catch a regression of the windowing. First commit of item 3: move the two functions into a detail header (or give them a "whole canvas" switch) and test the bounded result against the unbounded one on synthetic canvases with sprite pixels at the canvas edges, rivers whose nearest sea lies just inside and just outside the margin, and seams at the window border; a margin one pixel smaller must fail it. The GPU tests again ran on an unnamed adapter: the next render report names it (§1 point 9).
- **T11 stage 1 finish and T3 stage 3 interim (2026-10-21 k).** **T11 (3ccebb6..e73940e, PR #5 green): merged as daaa849.** (1) Measured on `main` and the branch, 1 and 16 threads, both scenes, three groups: the objects stage is 0.4-20 % faster, not slower (the keel is computed once per frame in the table instead of once per blit), the chunk mean −6 % .. +2 %; the bins and the shared lock stay. One run each on a non-idle machine: the direction is clear, the small numbers are noise. (2) Unit frames are keyed by (figure, frame index, group, colour, shadow), mirroring the decoder's own cache key, with a test that identical units share an id; frames without an identity fall back to the image address, which the table keeps alive, so it cannot be reused. Note for section 10.2 (sim units): the key assumes one frame set per zoom group, which holds for scenario units today; animated sim units (walk, work, fight, rotations) need the set in the key. (3) Section 12 folds C1, C2, C4, C5 in as decided and names C3 OQ-096. Not done by the executor: the full suite with the game files after the key change (only the related tests); CI's suite without game files is green. **T3 stage 3 interim (408cd46, d8e8b36, 6b4b032): reviewed and merged as c6cb39b via the integration PR #7 on top of T11, so the level-table refactor reaches `main` before T1f 2b-4 and T11 2a branch off.** `ZoomLevels` keeps the built groups as `kTile` / `kGroups` (scene content stays per built group) and stores derived levels as the source size with a power-of-two shift, so every ratio stays an exact integer fraction; `reduce_chunks_2x` is integer throughout, ties-up rounding stated, objects as the alpha-weighted mean of straight RGBA (premultiplied mean, un-premultiplied), ground and terrain over the land texels only, field cover/shore/depth means, flag bits 0-1 OR-ed and the slope averaged over water texels (condition 1, with its reason), `base` defined as absent at derived levels, objects then drawn over the water as pictures (condition 2's definition; its compositor test is still to come); `reduce_fs` repeats it operation for operation with exact unorm round trips and equals it byte for byte on noise chunks and on real group-2 chunks of two scenes over two levels, a shader mutation fails both tests; the pass is reachable only from a test entry point that reads back and waits, nothing on the window thread uses it yet. All five conditions are written into the design note. **CI caught one error the local gates could not:** `gpu.cpp` in the reduce pass added an unsigned offset to an iterator (`-Wsign-conversion` on Clang only; MSVC and GCC accept it, and the executor has no Clang locally); fixed by the orchestrator on the integration branch as e799691, PR #7 then green on all jobs. Until Clang is available locally, every branch gets its CI run before the report says "warning-free". **Gate gap:** the report says the GPU tests ran on "the default adapter" without naming it, and no iGPU run: §1 point 9 requires the adapter line; the next render report names it, and the iGPU run is part of the stage 3 measurements.
- **T3 stage 3 design note (2026-10-21, orchestrator review of `iso.md` "Continuous zoom, stage 3 design" at 2ebdf7f; the default flip 7687eb2 verified: `mode_explicit`, silent CPU fallback, usage, tests). Merged as 2d15db9 (PR #6 green). Design APPROVED with five conditions.** The core is right: zoom levels as a list (PLAN 0.1), levels 3+ derived by an exact 2x box reduction of the previous level on the GPU at upload, the shown level nearest in ratio so the resample scale stays within 0.69..1.45 and the composed grid within 1.45x the window at every zoom (the O(window) condition, with a sweep test), `ChunkKey` = (level, cx, cy) so the cache, prefetch, stand-ins and dissolve apply unchanged, a chunk drawn only when its four quadrants are in, source jobs by distance from the centre and capped with a reason, a CPU reference defined recursively that the GPU pass must equal exactly. Conditions: **(1) `field.flags` reduction:** a bitwise OR is right for the flag bits (0 original water, 1 river) and wrong for the slope quantity in bits 2-7 (OR of two 6-bit numbers is not a slope): reduce the slope field by its mean (or max; say which and why) and OR only the bits; the reference test must have a case with two different slopes. **(2) `base` at derived levels:** the compositor uses the objects' base plane for the water against objects; the note says `base` is not derived but not what the compositor then assumes (255 everywhere? the plane absent?): define it, and show in the GPU = CPU test at a derived level that objects standing in water look as the CPU reference says. **(3) The deepest levels stay resident:** a level-k chunk rebuilt from nothing needs 4^(k-2) group-2 chunks built on the CPU (the whole scene, 612 chunks for `Single_01`, for the minimum zoom: seconds of all cores), so derived levels whose whole-scene chunk count is below a bound (`resident_level_chunks`, e.g. 16, with the reason) are never evicted for the scene's lifetime; the overview is then instant after the first build. **(4) The first zoom-out fill:** while the coarser level is not built the stand-in comes from the finer level shrunk by nearest, i.e. aliased: state it in the note (the "never aliased" claim holds only once the coarser level exists) and measure the fill time (E) as planned, on 1 and 16 threads. **(5) The level-table refactor** (`kTile` to a per-scene `ZoomLevels`) touches every user of `kTile` / `kGroups`: its own commit with no behaviour change, every digest unchanged, before any derived level exists. Answers to the executor's questions: (1) the legacy river look at derived levels is accepted (a river is one or two pixels there; the owner can veto on the build); (2) the default minimum zoom "the scene fits the window" with `--min-zoom` as the floor is accepted as the design, the owner decides the default on the build (OQ-095 item 4); (3) a plain box mean, not gamma-aware, is accepted. Note for the sea pattern: if the group-2 period does not divide by 2^k, prefer a derived pattern whose period is rounded up with the tiling drift stated, as the note proposes.
- **T11 stage 1 (2026-10-21, orchestrator verification of bcbd967..547fb77 in the diff; PR #5). Correct, not merged yet: three things first.** Verified: `Scene::objects_draws` emits the records in the CPU's order (islands in layout order, each object's shadow before it, soldiers merged by their 1 x 1 key through the same `partition_point` walk, ships as an unclipped tail); `replay` opens one local canvas per clip segment and composites it as `render_with_terrain` does; `blit_sprite` is now a wrapper of `blit_sprite_keel` with the keel computed by `keel_rows` once per frame in the table instead of per blit (same overlap check first, same `contact` rule); the track flag equals the old `!shadow && !base.empty()`; records are written field by field, little-endian, 32 bytes, the ground record defined and layout-tested; bins as CSR over the image box clipped to the clip entry and the chunk; the residency model is deterministic (ordered containers), pins what this frame reads before evicting, evicts the least recently used unpinned layer of the class, packs tallest first with the id as the tie-break, `Deferred` on the upload cap and `Overflow` as the CPU-fallback counter; `FrameTable` ids are dense, never reused, interned on the worker outside the lock. The corpus test proves the list on every chunk of three scenes at three groups with ships on and off, byte for byte. GCC findings fixed; Clang only in CI (green). **Before the merge:** **(1) Measure the CPU builder**, which is now "build the list, replay it" for every chunk the viewer builds (PLAN 0.3): `bench_frame --chunk-profile 1,16 --groups 0,1,2` on the canyon island and `Single_24` on `main` and on the branch, the objects stage and the chunk mean per group into the README; the per-draw shared-lock on the frame table from 16 workers and the bins computed also for the `Cpu` builder (unused there) are the two suspects; if the objects stage is more than 5 % slower, skip the bins for `Cpu` and cache ids per worker, then measure again. **(2) The unit frame key:** `FrameKey::unit(index, group, shadow)` keys a frame by the unit's index in `Scene::units`, so two soldiers with the same figure, set, frame and colour get two ids and, in stage 2, two atlas copies: with thousands of units (the late-game case of T11 section 10.2) that is unbounded duplication. Key units by frame identity (figure entry, set, group, frame index, colour, shadow) as the design's 4.1 says, with a test that two identical units share an id. **(3) Section 12 of `gpu-chunks.md`** still lists C1-C5 as candidates and names OQ-095 as the highest number: fold the §4 decisions in (C1 exact compute pass, C2 objects first, C4 the switch names, C5 measure) and assign OQ-096 to C3, as the stage 0 review required (addition 4). The other stage 0 additions: (1) decode on the workers is done; (2) the initial atlas layer count from the scene's frame set and (3) the stand-in tests with both builders go into stage 2a. Known: the new corpus test renders whole scenes and joins the T10 memory-pressure flake list.
- **T3 stages 1-2 (2026-10-21, orchestrator verification of f273a9b..d810ba5 in the diff; two read-only reviewers of the executor, findings fixed in d810ba5). PR #4 green on all ten jobs; owner check: "zoom feels very smooth. no artifacts at all now while zooming and moving"; merged into `main` as 15a4aaa.** The deviation (compose on one group's grid as before, one resample pass; no fractional scale in any compositor shader) is accepted: it keeps every GPU = CPU test meaningful and the identity path byte-identical, at the cost of an extra full-window pass and a grid up to 1.45x the window per axis (2.1x the pixels at scale 0.69), which is what makes stage 3 a design question (see the entry: the grid must stay O(window)). Verified in the code: the easing is in the logarithm and frame-rate independent (`1 − e^(−3 dt / ease)`), the clock restarts from rest so an idle wait does not jump; the anchor invariant `p = cam + d / s` holds through the ease and across a group switch (camera scaled by the tile ratio, scale by its inverse); the group is chosen at rest too, with 5 % hysteresis and only when its sea pattern is ready, so a switch never waits for content on the window thread; panning divides by the scale; `resampled_view` rounds the grid to 64 px so the three grid textures are not recreated per frame, clamps the scale to [1/64, 64] and raises it to fit 8192 px; the uniform is 32 bytes as the WGSL struct; the pass reads with `textureLoad` only, so the filters are the same arithmetic on every adapter; the Box filter is bounded at 8 x 8 taps with the reason. Owner's log read: the single 34 ms hitch is the first frame (`sea 27.96`: the starting group's sea pattern built on the window thread at start-up, 97 chunks received in that interval), not a zoom event; the three later group switches show no `sea` step at all (worst busy 3.8 ms, `fluid` 1.6-2.7 ms on the larger grid), and the 9.8 ms gap is display wait (busy 0.9 ms). Not delivered from stage 1's text and moved to stage 3: `--zoom-factor` for the bench and `--png` with a CPU reference at a fractional scale, the README table (only "6 s at 500 fps" is reported), and the resample pass is outside the GPU timestamps. Executor's question (GPU tests skip in CI): correct, and it is now T13; until then the report states which GPU tests ran on which adapter (§1 point 9).
- **T1f items 1-2 (2026-10-21, orchestrator verification of 0f2583b..c14ee6a in the diff; the executor had two read-only reviewers whose findings are fixed in bd380ab and 84ec121). Merged into `main` as ae8b936 after PR #3 was green on all jobs.** Verified in the code, not only in the report: `SmoothSampler::smooth` repeats `smooth_height` term by term (the same weight polynomials, the same clamped 16 corners, the same accumulation order, then `height_noise`); `NoisePlane::at` equals `noise3` at an integer z because `lerp(a, b, 0) = a + (b − a) · 0 = a` exactly (and an FMA gives the same); `terrain_plane` repeats `terrain_height` (same `tx`/`ty`, `s`/`t`, corner order, both branches) and the old `terrain_at`'s two `nullopt` cases become `kNoTerrain`; the river masks skipped on a chunk without river sprite pixels are a no-op because `blurred_alpha` returns zeros for a blank canvas and `apply_fall_masks` then `continue`s at every pixel (`a = 0`, `bed = −2`; the `static_assert` ties it to the constants); the empty-cell proof holds as written (condition 1 covers the river branch that the `!sea_chunk` case can still enter; `river_level` returns nothing when all four taps are NaN; the B-spline weights are non-negative and sum to 1 for t in [0, 1); the noise is bounded below by −(2 + 0.6) / 2 · shore_noise; the 0.5 margin covers rounding); the shortcut index uses floor(x), floor(y) inside [0, w) × [0, h), the same tile `tile_ground` would use; samplers and the cell table are per call and per island, nothing shared between workers; `classify_cells` runs once per island inside the existing `call_once`, one byte per tile. Tests: golden digest of a synthetic two-island scene recorded with the pre-change sources, checked with and without the classes; six mutations each caught; 458 game islands with all sampled positions empty; scene digests unchanged. Result: 50.1 to 29.1 ms (canyon g0), 44.2 to 21.4 ms (`Single_24` g0), field + terrain + flow 68 % to 46 % and 67 % to 33 %. **Targets missed and accepted:** the rest is the exact per-pixel evaluation of sea and coast pixels, which only T11 section 10.1 removes; see the T1f entry for item 2b and the 16-thread rerun. Still open from the report: islands of width 1 are not tested (add a case in 2b).
- **T11 stage 0 (2026-10-21, orchestrator review of `docs/render/gpu-chunks.md` at f3546f7). Approved; stage 1 is READY.** The design is evidence-based (frame counts per scene and chunk measured with the Python references: at most 7.8 MiB of frames and 3,608 instances in one chunk, every original scenario's frames under 200 MiB for all groups), reuses the existing atlas and sprite path, keeps the CPU blitter as the oracle with a replay proof before any shader, and names its limits with reasons. Decisions on the candidates of section 12: **C1: the exact binned compute pass** for objects and base (tolerance 0; the blending pass stays the documented fallback only). The reasoning holds: fixed-function blending rounds where the CPU floors, compounding shadows and the per-island local canvas with the `base` reset cannot be repeated by a blend state. Two conditions: the per-pixel walk is measured on the Radeon iGPU as well as the 4090 in stage 2a with the GPU timestamps, and bins (`bin_px`) are tuned from that measurement, not guessed. **C2: objects first (stage 2a); the ground pass (2b) after T1f item 2 unless the T1f profile shows the ground stage above 20 % of the chunk time on its own.** **C4: `--chunk-build cpu|gpu` in the viewer and the bench, `ANNO_VIEW_GPU_CHUNKS` as the interim switch; the default flips to `gpu` with the GPU renderer only after the stage 2a owner check.** **C5: measure; a counted edge-pixel tolerance on the ground is acceptable only with the count in the test and the README.** **C3 goes to the owner as OQ-096** (atlas budget 512 MiB discrete / 256 MiB integrated proposed). Additions for stage 1: (1) move the frame decoding to the workers as designed, it is a hitch source today (decode on first use on the window thread); (2) size the initial atlas layer count from the scene's frame set at load (the counting exists), so the doubling copy of a 256 MiB atlas rarely happens in a frame, and if it does, record it in `GpuStats`; (3) the stand-in CPU reference and the GPU-equals-CPU stand-in tests run with both builders (with `GpuList` the CPU cache holds lists, not pixels: the stand-in source for the CPU reference must then come from a read-back or the test runs the CPU builder, say which); (4) fold these decisions into section 12 of the design and assign OQ-096 there. Nothing else to change.
- **T1e (2026-10-21, orchestrator verification of the twelve commits b0588c5..fa1a6ba in the diff; the executor had two read-only reviewers of its own and lists their findings as fixed). Merged into `main` as 0467a51; the owner check is complete.** Verified: the fade starts only over a placeholder an earlier frame drew (`it != end && !real`) and the placeholder is kept in `fade_under` and drawn under the dissolve (the executor found on the way that the open sea under a dissolve was otherwise the clear colour); the ground and sea pipelines are opaque again with `ground_fade_pipe` / `sea_fade_pipe` set once for the last draws of a pass; the fade clock is a steady clock of the compositor (`set_fade_clock`, tests step it); `shown_over_stand_in` is set by the compositor before `update_chunks` so the retain covers chunks not uploaded or dissolving; the margin of the current group is prefetched before the neighbours' views and `resident` is counted over the two lists; a jump of more than a view resets the velocity; open sea is counted by uncovered area in the view (`open_sea_px`); `build_ms_` uses a CAS loop; `replace_chunk` pools planes in the prefetch path; `--lookahead-chunks` bounded to 16 with the overflow reason. Measurements: plain pan 0 dissolves and 0 open sea at 1600 and 3200 px/s on both scenes, max frame 6.5 ms; pan while zooming still shows open sea by area in 74-201 of 1600 frames (up to 18 % of the view; T1d's "0 frames" was chunk-wise), all of it unbuilt sources: that is T1f/T11, as expected. Owner (2026-10-21): "the blue squares still appear, overall it improved a bit"; "fade feels fine, chunks appear while paused. terrain seems a bit behind in rendering while zooming in and out" (build latency of the new group plus the 85 ms wave-window rebuild per step, OQ-076; T1f/T11 and T3). Known: the full ctest with the game dir still hits the T10 `bad_alloc` for two tests that pass alone (T10b).
- **T1d (2026-10-20, two independent read-only reviewers, no builds; orchestrator verified the two decisive findings in the code). Not merged; continued as T1e.** Right: the cause analysis (build latency 100-250 ms per chunk, key-ordered queue, neighbours never uploaded), `lookahead_rect` (sign, units, clamp), the queue order visible < margin < neighbours with the whole queue replaced each frame (no starvation), the velocity scaling across a group change (no spike at a zoom step), the decay when the view stops, `trim` keeping exactly keep ∪ retain ∪ 48 nearest independent of speed, the prefetch gate (never in a frame where a visible chunk waits, one per frame, bounded by `prefetch_chunks`, evicted when out of `keep`), the piece record (`transparent` at byte 36, older records opaque), opaque output unchanged by the blend state (alpha exactly 1.0), fade bookkeeping (start on an upload of a visible key only, ended by a zoom step, eviction or completion), the measurements (open sea 0 frames in 36 pan runs, pop 12-31 % → 1.3-2.7 %, window thread max 4.3-5.9 ms). Wrong or missing, now T1e: (1) the fade starts for every chunk first uploaded while visible and the current group's margin is built but not uploaded ahead, so a *plain pan* dissolves every chunk entering at the screen edge for 150 ms from a 2x stand-in or the sea colour: a regression of the one thing the owner called "really smooth", invisible to the `--pop` metric; (2) the fade uses the pausable animation clock: a chunk uploaded while paused stays invisible for the whole pause; (3) blending on for every ground and sea draw, cost not measured on either GPU; (4) no retain of the stand-in's sources for the 150 ms once the real chunk exists; (5) the dissolve over a stand-in is tested by nothing (empty scene), the fade test checks "> 5 levels" not a value; (6) a view jump produces a bogus velocity of D / 0.15 px/s; (7) `open_sea_chunks` is per chunk, holes in a cover are not counted, so "open sea 0 frames" is true chunk-wise only; (8) the memory comment ("a few hundred MB") understates the counted worst case (up to 1.5 GB of kept chunks at 2560x1440 on a diagonal pan, far less in real scenes with empty sea chunks; bounded, independent of speed); (9) the seam test tolerates a one-destination-pixel shift for the three coarser-source pairs (zoom-in direction), so "no shift" is proven only to half a source pixel there; the shader is pinned to the CPU rule within 1 level by the earlier noise test, so a shader-only shift would be caught; accepted as is with the statement corrected in iso.md. Items 3 and 4 of T1d (river flip, wave window) left conditional: accepted, OQ-091 / OQ-076 stay open. **Process:** the executor did the task inline with no reviewers and asked for them: right call. **The owner's "still artifacts" log of the same evening was of the `main` build** (exe of 20:21 in `C:\Users\altin\anno1503\build`, T1c code; the T1d exe is of 20:58 in the T1d worktree), so it is not evidence against T1d; the owner checks after T1e with the command in T1e.
- **T1c (2026-10-20, orchestrator review of the nine commits f6be695..740fee8; the subagent reviewer was stopped by accident). Merged into `main`.** Verified in the diff: the pre-creation thread runs at normal priority and creates one plane per quiet frame from a token that `render()` hands over under the warm mutex (no lost wake-up), unpaced only until the first frame, so at most one allocation can overlap a window-thread upload; the refused-texture check compares the uncaptured-error counter and any error from either thread ends pre-creation with the plane dropped (documented, acceptable); `stand_in_retain` returns nothing unless stand-ins are on and a visible chunk is missing, so "off = old eviction" now holds; the idle budget (`GpuLimits::idle_plane_budget_mb` 256, reason in the header) bounds pooled plus pre-created planes with the pool counted first; `take_warm` holds the lock for a push or pop only; the per-frame "chunk(s) received" write is gone from the frame path (only failure messages and hitch lines above the threshold remain). Placeholders unchanged with a measured reason (0.13-0.32 ms per zoom step, pixel semantics of the sea pattern); accepted for now, revisit if T1d's cross-fade changes the chunk bind group anyway. Idle bench (24 rows, 5 rounds, load 2-36 %): stand-ins cost p50 0, p95 +0.1-0.2 ms, max 2.0-3.1 ms; the T1b regression was load. Remaining known limits: `kPoolPlanes` 192 is still a constexpr (OQ-090), real chunk textures of all groups are bounded only through the CPU cache's trim, prewarm size does not follow a resize (OQ-093). Full ctest on the branch hit the T10 bad_alloc under `-j 16` (three tests pass alone); CI runs after the push.
- **T1b / T6 / T7 / T10 (2026-10-20, three independent reviewers, read-only, no builds).** T6 merged (`cef83b8`): survey definitions match `river_qa`, totals cross-check, the ctest case runs only the unit tests (7 s), docs lead with the 17.5 % cost and say "templates only"; low fixes in T6b. T7: code correct and hostile-input safe, exit criterion compile-time enforced; docs stale and the wire version undecided: T7b. T10: bound and log fine, wait/retry silent with a dead tail, and the executor's objection to CTest properties was wrong (doctest discovery can be split by test spec): T10b. T1b: feature and instrumentation sound, but the prewarm thread added in T1b creates a priority inversion on the device allocator lock, the "device refuses a texture" fix cannot fire under wgpu-native, `retain` runs on every path, and nothing bounds VRAM: T1c. The reviewer's best explanation for the owner's 26-39 ms frames is the per-frame `chunk(s) received` console write on the window thread, which `bench_frame` never does; T1c tests that first. The executor's "acceptance holds" was measured at 16-46 % load; not accepted as evidence. The `--water enhanced` fix means every earlier "enhanced mode" observation by the owner was in original water unless `E` was pressed.
- **T1 (2026-10-20, not merged; continued as T1b).** The design is right: the compositor draws parts of the nearest built group's chunks through a per-draw piece uniform with dynamic offsets, chosen per part so holes are filled from the next group, with a CPU reference and GPU-equals-CPU tests, mutation-checked; off switch in both the viewer and the bench; defaults of the CPU path untouched so digests hold. Read `gpu.cpp`: per-frame work is O(visible chunks × groups), no new fixed limits. Not accepted: (1) the owner's run shows 26–39 ms frames after a zoom that the instrumentation does not explain; (2) the benchmark ran on a loaded machine and the p99/max after-numbers are worse; (3) PNG evidence predates the last fix; (4) an "unexplained" `div_euclid` note left standing (the function is correct for the stated input); (5) the artifact question to the owner is right but should come with the two isolating switches and the three concrete suspects (legacy-river flip on stand-ins, wave-window rebuild, nearest-neighbour blockiness). "Not smooth" is expected: T2/T3 are the smoothing steps and stay locked.
